Equivariant scaling asymptotics for Poisson and Szegő kernels on Grauert tube boundaries
Simone Gallivanone, Roberto Paoletti

Abstract
Let ( M , κ ) (M,\kappa) be a closed and connected real-analytic Riemannian manifold, acted upon by a compact Lie group of isometries G G . We consider the following two kinds of equivariant asymptotics along a fixed Grauer tube boundary X τ X^{\tau} of ( M , κ ) (M,\kappa) . 1): Given the induced unitary representation of G G on the eigenspaces of the Laplacian of ( M , κ ) (M,\kappa) , these split over the irreducible representations of G G . On the other hand, the eigenfunctions of the Laplacian of ( M , κ ) (M,\kappa) admit a simultaneous complexification to some Grauert tube. We study the asymptotic concentration along X τ X^{\tau} of the complexified eigenfunctions pertaining to a fixed isotypical component. 2): There are furthermore an induced action of G G as a group of CR and contact automorphisms on X τ X^{\tau} , and a corresponding unitary representation on the Hardy space H ⁡ ( X τ ) H(X^{\tau}) . The action of G G on X τ X^{\tau} commutes with the homogeneous ‘geogesic flow’ and the representation on the Hardy space commutes with the elliptic self-adjoint Toeplitz operator induced by the generator of the goedesic flow. Hence each eigenspace of the latter also splits over the irreducible representations of G G . We study the asymptotic concentration of the eigenfunctions in a given isotypical component. We also give some applications of these asymptotics.



1
Introduction

Let
M
be a compact and connected
d
-dimensional real-analytic (in the following,
\mathcal{C}^{\varpi}
) manifold. There exists an essentially
unique complex manifold
(\tilde{M},J)
, the so-called
Bruhat-Whitney complexification
of
M
, in which
M
embeds as totally real submanifold (i.e.
T_{x}M\cap J_{x}\left(T_{x}M\right)=\{0\}
for any
x\in M
)

[
BW
]
. More precisely,
(\tilde{M},J)

is uniquely determined as a germ of complex manifold along
M
;
in particular, since both
(\tilde{M},J)
and
(\tilde{M},-J)
are
complexifications of
M
, there is an anti-holomorphic involution

\sigma:\tilde{M}\rightarrow\tilde{M}
having
M
as its fixed locus.

As proved and discussed in
[
GS-1991
]
,
[
GS-1992
]
,
[
Lem
]
,
[
LS
]
,

[
SZ02
]
(see also
[
PW-1989
]
and
[
PW-1991
]
),
the choice of a
\mathcal{C}^{\varpi}

Riemannian metric
\kappa
on
M
singles out a unique

\mathcal{C}^{\varpi}
exhaustion function on
\tilde{M}
(perhaps after passing to a smaller
tubular neighbourhood of
M
in
\tilde{M}
), with the following
properties:

1.

\rho:\tilde{M}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
, and

M=\rho^{-1}(0)
;

2.

\rho=\rho\circ\sigma
;

3.

\rho
is strictly plurisubharmonic, i.e. it induces
a Kähler form
\Omega
and a Riemannian metric

\hat{\kappa}
on
\tilde{M}
given by

\Omega:=\imath\partial\overline{\partial}\rho,\qquad\hat{\kappa}:=\Omega(\cdot,J(\cdot)).

(1)

4.

(M,\kappa)
is a Riemannian submanifold of

(\tilde{M},\hat{\kappa})
;

5.

on
\tilde{M}\setminus M
the function
\sqrt{\rho}

satisfies the complex homogeneous Monge-Ampère equation:

\mathrm{det}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\sqrt{\rho}}{\partial z_{i}\partial\overline{z}_{j}}\right)=0.

Without pretense of completeness, we refer the reader to
[
B
]
,

[
BH
]
,
[
G
]
,
[
Z-2007
]
,
[
Z-2012
]
,
[
Z-2020
]
,
[
CR1
]
and
[
CR2
]

for a wider foundational discussion.

Let
\tau_{0}^{2}:=\sup(\rho)
; then

\tau_{0}\in(0,+\infty]
, and
for any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})

the (open)
Grauert tube of radius
\tau

and its boundary are given by

\tilde{M}^{\tau}:=\rho^{-1}\big([0,\tau^{2})\big),\qquad X^{\tau}:=\partial\tilde{M}^{\tau}=\left\{\sqrt{\rho}=\tau\right\}.

(2)

Grauert tubes have been studied extensively in recent years,
both for their intrinsic geometric interest and in relation
to the following fundamental holomorphic extension property of the eigenfunctions
of the non-negative Laplacian
\Delta
of
(M,\kappa)
,
which was first discovered by Bouted de Monvel

[
BdM-1978
]
; this foundational result was also proved
and analyzed in
[
Leb
]
,
[
S-2014
]
,
[
S-2015
]
,
[
Z-2020
]

(see furthermore the discussion in
[
GS-1992
]
).
We give the formulation in
[
Leb
]
.

Let us denote by

0=\mu_{1}^{2}<\mu_{2}^{2}<\cdots\uparrow+\infty\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mu_{j}\geq 0

(3)

the distinct eigenvalues of
\Delta
; for every
j=1,2,\cdots
,
let

W_{j}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)
be the (finite-dimensional)
eigenspace for the eigenvalue

\mu_{j}^{2}
, and let
(\varphi_{j,k})_{k=1}^{n_{j}}
a real orthonormal basis of

W_{j}
.
For
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, let us set

\langle\mu\rangle:=\left(1+\mu^{2}\right)^{1/2}
.

Theorem 1
.

(Boutet de Monvel)
There exists
\tau_{1}\in(0,\tau_{0}]
such that the following holds:

1.

every eigenfunction

\varphi
of
\Delta

admits a holomorphic extension
\tilde{\varphi}
to

\tilde{M}^{\tau_{1}}
, hence
for any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{1})

the restriction

\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}:=\left.\tilde{\varphi}\right|_{X^{\tau}}

to
X^{\tau}
is a CR function;

2.

the sequence of functions

\left(e^{-\tau\,\epsilon_{j}}\,\langle\mu_{j}\rangle^{(d-1)/4}\,\tilde{\varphi}_{j,k}^{\tau}\right)_{j\geq 1,\,k=1,\ldots,n_{j}}

is a Riesz basis of
H(X^{\tau})
.

If
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
on
X^{\tau}
we have the following geometric structures
(see §3.2 of
[
P-2024
]
, to which we conform for conventions and notation):

1.

a contact form
\alpha^{\tau}
, given a follows: if

\jmath^{\tau}:X^{\tau}\hookrightarrow\tilde{M}
is the inclusion,

\alpha^{\tau}:={\jmath^{\tau}}^{*}(\alpha),\qquad\text{where}\qquad\alpha:=\Im(\partial\rho);

(4)

2.

a CR structure

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}:=TX^{\tau}\cap J(TX^{\tau})=\ker(\alpha^{\tau})

(5)

(the maximal complex sub-vector bundle of the tangent bundle
TX^{\tau}
);

3.

the closed symplectic cone sprayed by
\alpha^{\tau}

in the complement of the
zero section
X^{\tau}_{0}
of the cotangent bundle
T^{\vee}X^{\tau}

of
X^{\tau}
:

\Sigma^{\tau}:=\left\{\left(x,r\,\alpha^{\tau}_{x}\right)\,:\,x\in X^{\tau},\,r>0\right\}\subseteq T^{\vee}X^{\tau}\setminus X^{\tau}_{0};

(6)

4.

the smooth vector field
\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}^{\tau}\in\mathfrak{X}(X^{\tau})
given by the restriction to
X^{\tau}
of the Hamiltonian vector field
\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}
of
\sqrt{\rho}

with respect to
\Omega
;

5.

the Reeb vector field
\mathcal{R}^{\tau}
of
(X^{\tau},\alpha^{\tau})
,
given by

\mathcal{R}^{\tau}:=-\frac{1}{\tau}\,\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}^{\tau};

(7)

6.

the real vector sub-bundle
\mathcal{T}^{\tau}

of the tangent bundle
TX^{\tau}
generated
\mathcal{R}^{\tau}
, so that

TX^{\tau}=\mathcal{T}^{\tau}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\tau}

(8)

(we shall occasionally refer to
\mathcal{T}^{\tau}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
as, respectively, the
\alpha^{\tau}
-vertical and
the

\alpha^{\tau}
-horizontal tangent bundles of
X^{\tau}
);

7.

a ‘Riemannian’  volume form
\mathrm{vol}^{R}_{X^{\tau}}
,
given by the contraction of the gradient of
\sqrt{\rho}
with the
symplectic volume form of
(\tilde{M}\setminus M,\Omega)
, restricted to

X^{\tau}
.

In addition,
on
X^{\tau}
we have the following analytic structures:

1.

the Hardy space
H(X^{\tau}):=\mathrm{ker}(\overline{\partial}_{b})\subseteq L^{2}(X^{\tau})
;

2.

the corresponding Szegő projector and its distributional kernel,
the so-called Szegő kernel:

\Pi^{\tau}:L^{2}(X^{\tau})\longrightarrow H(X^{\tau}),\qquad\Pi^{\tau}(\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau})

(9)

(what follows is pivoted on the microlocal description of

\Pi^{\tau}
as a Fourier integral operator in
[
BdM-S
]
);

3.

a privileged collection of CR functions (elements of

H(X^{\tau})
),
given by the restrictions
\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}

to
X^{\tau}
of the holomorphic extensions
\varphi

of the eigenfunctions of
\Delta
(if
\tau\in(0,\tau_{1})
);

4.

the self-adjoint first-order elliptic
Toeplitz operator

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}:=\Pi^{\tau}\circ D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}\circ\Pi^{\tau},\qquad\text{where}\qquad D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}:=\imath\,\upsilon^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}.

(10)

In fact, the principal symbol of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

(i.e., the restriction to
\Sigma^{\tau}

in (
6
) of the principal symbol of

D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
) is strictly positive;
hence the spectrum of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

is discrete
and accumulates only at
+\infty

[
BdM-G
]
.

In a series of papers,
Zelditch has laid the foundations and paved the way for the asymptotic study
of both the complexified eigenfunctions of the Laplacian and of the
eigenfunctions of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

(see e.g.
[
Z-2007
]
,
[
Z-2012
]
,
[
Z-2014
]
,
[
Z-2017
]
,
[
Z-2020
]
).
These asymptotics are encrypted in certain ‘tempered’
complexified
spectral projectors (in the case of the Laplacian) and in a smoothed
spectral projector (in the case of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
),
whose definitions we briefly recall.

In the case of the Laplacian, the asymptotic distribution of the
complexified eigenfunctions of
\Delta
, restricted to
X^{\tau}
,
is encapsulated in the asymptotics for

\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
of
the tempered complexified projector kernels

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\,\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau})

given by

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\mu_{j})e^{-2\tau\mu_{j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,k}(x)\,\overline{\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,k}(y)},

(11)

where
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})

(the space of compactly supported smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}
)
and

\hat{\chi}
is its Fourier transform.
Since the restrictions
(\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,k})_{k}
are not
orthonormal, the inner sum may not be interpreted as a genuine
projector, even in a rescaled sense.

Similarly, let

\lambda_{1}^{\tau}<\lambda_{2}^{\tau}<\cdots\uparrow+\infty
denote the distinct eigenvalues
of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
, and for each

j
let
H(X^{\tau})_{j}\subset H(X^{\tau})
be the eigenspace of

\lambda_{j}^{\tau}
. For
j=1,2,\ldots
, let

(\rho_{j,k}^{\tau})_{k=1}^{\ell_{j}^{\tau}}
be an orthonormal basis of

H(X^{\tau})_{j}
. The asymptotic distribution of the

\rho_{j,k}^{\tau}
’s and of the
\lambda_{j}^{\tau}
’s is probed
by smoothed projection kernels of the form

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\,\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau})

given by

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\chi}\left(\lambda-\lambda_{j}^{\tau}\right)\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{j}^{\tau}}\rho_{j,k}(x)\,\overline{\rho_{j,k}(y)}.

(12)

This is the Schwartz kernel of the smoothed projector

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\,\lambda}:=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hat{\chi}\left(\lambda-\lambda_{j}^{\tau}\right)\Pi^{\tau}_{j},

(13)

where
\Pi^{\tau}_{j}:L^{2}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow H(X^{\tau})_{j}
is the orthogonal
projector.

Remark 2
.

In the following, for notational simplicity we shall omit the suffix
\tau
from the
spectral data
\lambda_{j}^{\tau}
and
\ell_{j}^{\tau}
.

While (
11
) is peculiar to the
Grauert tube setting, (
12
) is the
counterpart of classically studied kernels in the spectral theory
of pseudodifferential operators (see e.g.
[
DG
]
and
[
GrSj
]
);
in the Toeplitz setting, in the geometric context of positive line bundles and with an emphasis
on local scaling asymptotics, they have been used in
[
P-2009
]
,
[
P-2010
]
,
[
P-2011
]
,

[
P1-2012
]
,
[
P-2017
]
,
[
P-2018
]
,
[
ZZ-2018
]
,
[
ZZ1-2019
]
,

[
ZZ2-2019
]
.

In two recent papers (
[
CR1
]
and
[
CR2
]
),
Chang and Rabinowitz have
made groundbreaking progress in pushing forward the analogy
between the line bundle and the Grauert tube settings, providing
local scaling asymptotics for
(
11
) and
(
12
) bearing a striking similarity with
the local scaling asymptotics for the
Fourier components of Szegő kernels
in the line bundle setting (
[
Z-1998
]
,
[
BSZ
]
and
[
SZ02
]
).
Besides the description of
\Pi^{\tau}
as a Fourier integral
operator in
[
BdM-S
]
, which underpins the whole theory,
the approach of Chang and Rabinowitz in based on the following
two pillars. The first is the description, due to Zelditch,
of certain 1-parameter groups of Toeplitz operators
as ‘dynamical Toeplitz operators’, which goes back to

[
Z-1997
]
and is pervasive in his treatment of the subject.
The second is a clever use of the ‘Heisenberg local coordinates’
adapted to a hypersurface in a complex manifold introduced by
Folland and Stein in
[
FS1
]
and
[
FS2
]
. The approach of
Chang and Rabinowitz has been reviewed and elaborated in

[
P-2024
]
, where a refinement of their asymptotic
expansions is given in the near-diagonal case, where

\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}\big((-\epsilon,\epsilon)\big)
.

The aim of this paper is to extend the results of
[
P-2024
]

to the (off-diagonal and) equivariant setting.
Suppose given a smooth action

\mu:G\times M\rightarrow M
of a

d_{G}
-dimensional
compact Lie group
G
as a group of
symmetries on
(M,\kappa)
.
Then there are naturally induced unitary representations of

G
on
L^{2}(M)
and
H(X^{\tau})
, commuting with
\Delta
and

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
, respectively.
Therefore, for every
j\geq 1

both the eigenspaces
W_{j}\subset L^{2}(M)
of
\Delta
and

H(X^{\tau})_{j}\subset H(X^{\tau})
are invariant.

Notation 3
.

Let
\hat{G}
denote the unitary dual of
G
; for every

\nu\in\hat{G}
,
we shall adopt the following notation.

1.

V_{\nu}
is the representation space
of
\nu
, and
\dim(\nu):=\dim(V_{\nu})
;

2.

\Xi_{\nu}:G\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is the character of
\nu
.

3.

If
G
is unitarily represented on a Hilbert space

H
,
H_{\nu}\subseteq H
will
denote the
\nu
-th isotypical component of
H
.

By the Theorem
of Peter and Weyl (see e.g.
[
S-95
]
)
there are unitary equivariant Hilbert direct sum decompositions

L^{2}(M)=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\hat{G}}L^{2}(M)_{\nu},\quad W_{j}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\hat{G}}W_{j,\nu},

(14)

where
W_{j,\nu}:=W_{j}\cap L^{2}(M)_{\nu}
,
and similarly

H(X^{\tau})=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\hat{G}}H(X^{\tau})_{\nu},\quad H(X^{\tau})_{j}=\bigoplus_{\nu\in\hat{G}}H(X^{\tau})_{j,\nu},

(15)

where

H(X^{\tau})_{j,\nu}:=H(X^{\tau})_{j}\cap H(X^{\tau})_{\nu}
.

For every
(j,\nu)
such that
W_{j,\nu}\neq(0)
, let

(\varphi_{j,\nu,k})_{k=1}^{n_{j,\nu}}
be a real orthonormal
basis of
W_{j,\nu}
. The equivariant analogue of
(
11
) is the smooth kernel

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau})

given by

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\mu_{j})e^{-2\tau\mu_{j}}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j,\nu}}\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(x)\,\overline{\tilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(y)}.

(16)

Similarly, for every
(j,\nu)
for which

H(X^{\tau})_{j,\nu}\neq(0)
,
let
(\rho_{j,\nu,k})_{k=1}^{\ell_{j,\nu}}

be an orthonormal basis of
H(X^{\tau})_{j,\nu}
. The
equivariant analogue of (
12
) is the
smooth kernel
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau})
given by

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\chi}\left(\lambda-\lambda_{j}\right)\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{j,\nu}}\rho_{j,\nu,k}(x)\,\overline{\rho_{j,\nu,k}(y)}.

(17)

Thus
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)
is the Schwartz kernel
of the smoothed projector

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}:=\sum_{j\geq 1}\hat{\chi}\left(\lambda-\lambda_{j}\right)\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{j,\nu}}\,\Pi^{\tau}_{\nu,j}=P_{\nu}\circ\Pi_{\chi,\lambda},

(18)

where
\Pi^{\tau}_{\nu,j}:L^{2}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow H(X^{\tau})_{j,\nu}

and
P_{\nu}:L^{2}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow L^{2}(X^{\tau})_{\nu}

are the orthogonal projectors.

We shall provide local scaling asymptotics for (
16
) and
(
17
).
Unlike
[
P-2024
]
, we are not assuming here that

\chi
is supported near the origin; in the special
action-free
case, we shall thus recover a refinement of the near-graph
scaling asymptotics of Chang and Rabinowitz in
[
CR2
]
.

The smooth action
\mu:G\times M\rightarrow M

extends (perhaps after decreasing
\tau_{0}
) to an
action
\tilde{\mu}:G\times\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}\rightarrow\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}
of
G
as a group of biholomorphisms.
Since
\mu
is isometric for
(M,\kappa)
,
\tilde{\mu}

preserves
\rho
.
Thus
G
acts as a group of Hamiltonian automorphisms
of the Kähler manifold
(\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}},J,\Omega)
,
with a moment map
\Phi:\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}

vanishing identically on
M
.

Since
\tilde{\mu}
preserves
\rho
,
it also preserves every
X^{\tau}
, with its
CR and contact structure, for
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
;
let
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}:G\times X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}

be the resulting contact and CR action.
Let us set

Z:=\Phi^{-1}(0)\subseteq\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}},\qquad Z^{\tau}:=Z\cap X^{\tau}.

(19)

What follows depends on the following hypothesis.

Assumption 4
.

We shall assume that:

1.

Z\setminus M\neq\emptyset
(hence
Z^{\tau}\neq\emptyset
for every

\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
);

2.

\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
is locally free on
Z^{\tau}
.

Remark 5
.

The second requirement means that
0\in\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
is
a regular value of
\left.\Phi\right|_{\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}\setminus M}
.
It is satisfied
a fortiori
if
\mu
itself is locally free.
Under Assumption
4
,
d>d_{G}

(see Corollary
55
below).

Let
\Gamma^{\tau}:\mathbb{R}\times X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}

denote the flow
of
\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}
(
\Gamma^{\tau}
will be referred to -
with some abuse of language - as the ‘homogeneous geodesic flow’,
since it is intertwined with the
latter by the imaginary time exponential map - see
[
GS-1992
]
,
[
LS
]
,
[
GLS
]
).
Since
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
and
\Gamma^{\tau}
commute,
there is a product action of
G\times\mathbb{R}
on
X^{\tau}
;
our first result is that (
16
) and
(
17
) asymptotically concentrate
near certain compact loci
\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}\subset X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}
determined
by
\chi
, the moment map, and the orbits of the latter action.

Definition 6
.

If
x\in X^{\tau}
and
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, we set

x^{G\times\chi}:=\left\{\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\,:\,g\in G,\,t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\right\}.

We then pose

\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}:=\left\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in Z^{\tau}\times Z^{\tau}\,:\,x_{1}\in x_{2}^{G\times\chi}\right\}.

We also set

x^{\chi}:=\left\{\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\,:\,t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\right\}.

Theorem 7
.

For any
C,\,\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, we have

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right),

uniformly for

\max\left\{\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x,y^{G\times\chi}\right),\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x,Z^{\tau}\right)\right\}\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}.

We are thus led to consider the asymptotics of

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y)
and

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y)
when
(x,y)
ranges in a
shrinking neighourhood of a fixed pair

(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
.
As in
[
P-2024
]
,
the asymptotics in point will be formulated in suitable systems
of local coordinates at points
x\in X^{\tau}
, called
normal
Heisenberg local coordinates
(in the following, NHLC’s),
which are a slight specialization
of the Heisenberg local coordinates in
[
CR1
]
and
[
CR2
]

(see §3.3 of
[
P-2024
]
).

NHLC’s centered at
x\in X^{\tau}
will be written, in additive notation,
as

x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})
, where
(\theta,\mathbf{v})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
belongs to a neighbourhood of the origin.
In terms of (
8
), we have

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\right|_{x}=\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x)\in\mathcal{T}_{x}^{\tau},\qquad\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\mathbf{v}}\right|_{x}\in\mathcal{H}_{x}^{\tau}\quad\forall\,\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}.

(20)

Let us fix
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
and NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}

at
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
. We shall work in rescaled coordinates and set

x_{j,\lambda}:=x_{j}+\left(\frac{\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{v}_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\qquad(j=1,2).

(21)

1.1
Scaling asymptotics in the action-free case

To fix ideas and ease of exposition,
let us first consider separately
the action-free case (i.e. where
G
is trivial);
we shall write
x^{\chi}
for
x^{G\times\chi}
.
In this case,
Z^{\tau}=X^{\tau}
and by
Theorem
7
we have

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x,y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x,y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right),

uniformly for

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x,y^{\chi}\right)\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}.

We shall present near-graph
scaling asymptotics
for
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})
and

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

refining those in
[
CR2
]
, where rescaling is according to
Heisenberg type; furthermore, we shall provide an explicit description
of the leading order term and an estimate on the degree
of the polynomials in the rescaled variables intervening in the
lower order terms of the asymptotic expansions.

The leading order term describing the exponential decay in the
scaling asymptotics of
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})
and

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

depends on a real-quadratic complex valued form on the vector
subspace

\mathcal{H}_{x_{1}}^{\tau}\times\mathcal{H}_{x_{2}}^{\tau}\subseteq T_{x_{1}}X^{\tau}\times T_{x_{2}}X^{\tau}
. To describe the latter
form, we need a brief digression.

Definition 8
.

Given a symplectic matrix
A\in\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
, we shall set

A_{c}:=\mathcal{W}\,A\,\mathcal{W}^{-1}\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mathcal{W}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,\begin{pmatrix}I_{d-1}&\imath\,I_{d-1}\\
I_{d-1}&-\imath\,I_{d-1}\end{pmatrix}.

Then

A_{c}=\begin{pmatrix}P&Q\\
\overline{Q}&\overline{P}\end{pmatrix},

(22)

where
P
is invertible and
\|P\,\mathbf{z}\|\geq\|\mathbf{z}\|
,
\forall\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
(§4.1 of
[
F
]
).

Definition 9
.

Let us identify
\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\cong\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
in the usual
norm-preserving manner, so that if
Z_{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
corresponds
to
\mathbf{v}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
then

h_{0}(Z_{1},Z_{2})=g_{0}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})-\imath\,\omega_{0}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}),\qquad(\mathbf{v}_{1},\,\mathbf{v}_{2}\in\mathbb{C}^{d-1}),

where
h_{0}
,
g_{0}
, and
\omega_{0}
denote the standard
Hermitian, Euclidean, and symplectic structures, respectively.
Given
A\in\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
, let us define

\Psi_{A}:\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

(or, equivalently,
\Psi_{A_{c}}:\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\times\overline{\mathbb{C}}^{d-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
)
as follows.
With
P
and
Q
as in Definition
8
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{A}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})=\Psi_{A_{c}}(Z_{1},Z_{2})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\left(Z_{1}^{\dagger}\,\overline{Q}\,P^{-1}\,Z_{1}+2\,\overline{Z}_{2}^{\dagger}\,P^{-1}\,Z_{1}-\overline{Z}_{2}^{\dagger}\,P^{-1}\,Q\,\overline{Z}_{2}-\|Z_{1}\|^{2}-\|Z_{1}\|^{2}\right).

Remark 10
.

\Psi_{A}
plays an important role in the theory
of the metaplectic representation (see the discussions
in §4 of
[
F
]
,

[
D
]
,
[
ZZ-2018
]
).

Definition 11
.

If
(V,h^{\prime})
is a Hermitian complex
k
-dimensional vector space,
so that
h=g_{h}-\imath\,\omega_{h}
, where
g_{h}:=\Re(h)
and
\omega_{h}:=\Im(h)
are,
respectively, an Euclidean product and a
symplectic bilinear form on
V
.
We define
\psi_{2}^{\omega_{h}}:V\times V\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
by

\psi_{2}^{\omega_{h}}(v,v^{\prime}):=h(v,v^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|v\|_{h}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|v^{\prime}\|_{h}^{2}=-\imath\,\omega_{h}(v,v^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|v-v^{\prime}\|_{h}^{2},

where
\|v\|_{h}:=\sqrt{h(v,v)}
.

Notation 12
.

When
(V,h)=\left(\mathbb{C}^{k},h_{0}\right)
, where
h_{0}
is the
standard Hermitian product, we shall also view
\psi_{2}:=\psi_{2}^{\omega_{0}}
as
being defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2\,k}\times\mathbb{R}^{2k}
, where

\mathbb{R}^{2k}\cong\mathbb{C}^{k}
in the standard manner.
If
\mathbf{v}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\,k}
,

j=1,2
, corresponds to
Z_{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{k}

under the previous identification, we shall equivalently
write
\psi_{2}(Z_{1},Z_{2})
as
\psi_{2}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})

to emphasize the symplectic structure.

Remark 13
.

Let us identify the unitary group
U(d-1)
as the maximal compact subgroup

\hat{U}(d-1):=\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)\cap O(2d-2)
of

\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
in the standard manner.
Then
A\in\hat{U}(d-1)\leqslant\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
if and only if
in (
22
) we have

Q=0
and
P\in U(d-1)\leqslant\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbb{C}}(d-1)
.
Hence if
A\in\hat{U}(d-1)
then

\displaystyle\Psi_{A}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{Z}_{2}^{\dagger}\,P^{-1}\,Z_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|Z_{1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|Z_{1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{0}(Z_{1},P\,Z_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|Z_{1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|Z_{1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(Z_{1},P\,Z_{2})=\psi_{2}(\mathbf{v}_{1},A\,\mathbf{v}_{2})=\psi_{2}(A^{-1}\,\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}).

Remark 14
.

Let us identify
T_{x}X^{\tau}\cong\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}

through NHLC’s at
x
,
hence
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}\cong\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\cong\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
. Then
the previous invariants may be viewed as defined on

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
, and it is natural to expect that they have
an intrinsic geometric meaning. This is so, but the
symplectic and Euclidean pairings
on
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
corresponding
to
\omega_{0}=-\Im(h_{0})
and
g_{0}=\Re(h_{0})
are not

\Omega_{x}
and
\hat{\kappa}_{x}
, but rather their halves

\omega_{x}:=\frac{1}{2}\,\Omega_{x}
and
\tilde{\kappa}_{x}:=\frac{1}{2}\,\hat{\kappa}_{x}
(see
[
P-2024
]
).

Let us return to our geometric setting and consider,
in the action-free case, a pair
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
.
Hence there exists
t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
such that

x_{1}=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})
. It turns out that if

\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
is sufficiently small (shorter than
2\,\epsilon

for some
\epsilon>0
, say), then for
any

(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
there exists a
unique

t_{1}=t_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
such that

x_{1}=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2})

(see Lemma
57
below). If furthermore
NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
are chosen at
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, there is a unique

B=B_{x_{1},x_{2}}\in\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
such that

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\big(x_{1}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)=x_{2}+\big(\theta+R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\,B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big),

(23)

where
R_{k}
(respecively,
\mathbf{R}_{k}
) denotes, here and in the following,
a generic real-valued (respectively, vector-valued)
function on an open neighbourhood of the origin of some
Euclidean space, vanishing to
k
-th order at the origin;
(
23
)
is a special case of Lemma
67
below.

Theorem 15
.

Assume that

\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
is sufficiently small and

x_{1}\in x_{2}^{\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}
. Let

t_{1}=t_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})
be as above, and let
B=B_{x_{1},x_{2}}

be as in (
23
).
Suppose
C>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
.
Then, uniformly for

(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in T_{x_{j}}X^{\tau}
with

\|(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
, there
are asymptotic expansions

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})+\Psi_{B^{-1}}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right]}\cdot e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})}\cdot\left[\chi(t_{1})+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)\right],

\displaystyle P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2\,\pi}\right)^{d-1}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})+\Psi_{B^{-1}}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right]}\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})}\cdot\left[\chi(t_{1})+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,\widetilde{F}_{k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)\right],

where
e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})},\,e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})}\in S^{1}
and

F_{k}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)
,
\widetilde{F}_{k}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)
are
polynomials in the rescaled variables of
degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
.

When
x_{1}=x_{2}
and
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}\big((-\epsilon,\epsilon)\big)
for suitably small
\epsilon>0
then

M=I_{2d-2}
,
t_{1}=0
and
\theta^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})=\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})=0
. Thus we recover the near-diagonal
scaling asymptotic
in
[
P-2024
]
. Furthermore, we recover the near-graph
scaling asymptotics of
[
CR2
]
,
with an explicit
determination of the leading factor,
by fixing

(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})
and rescaling according to
Heisenberg type (that is, in the form

(\theta_{j}/\lambda,\mathbf{v}_{j}/\sqrt{\lambda})
).

1.2
Scaling asymptotics in the general equivariant case

In order to state our results in the general equivariant setting,
we need to introduce a finer decomposition of
T_{x}X^{\tau}
,
valid at
x\in Z^{\tau}
, than
the one dictated by
\alpha^{\tau}
in
(
8
); this decomposition depends on

\mu^{\tau}
and
\Phi
, and is the analogue of the decomposition used in

[
P-2008
]
in the line bundle setting.

Notation 16
.

For any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
(the Lie algebra of
G
)
we shall denote by
\xi_{X^{\tau}}
the induced vector field
on
X^{\tau}
, and for any
x\in X^{\tau}
we shall denote
by

\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x)\subseteq T_{x}X^{\tau}
the tangent space
at
x
to the
G
-orbit through
x
.

Remark 17
.

Suppose
x\in Z^{\tau}
. Then

1.

\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x)\subseteq\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
;

2.

since
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}=T_{x}X^{\tau}\cap J_{x}(T_{x}X^{\tau})
,
we also have

J_{x}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x)\big)\subset\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
;

3.

under Assumption
4
,

\dim\big(\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x)\big)=d_{G}
.

Definition 18
.

Suppose
x\in Z^{\tau}
. We set

T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau}:=\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x),\quad T_{x}^{t}X^{\tau}:=J_{x}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{X^{\tau}}(x)\big),\quad T_{x}^{h}X^{\tau}:=\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}\cap\left(T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{t}_{x}X^{\tau}\right)^{\perp},

where
\perp
denotes the Hermitian (equivalently,
symplectic or Riemannian) orthocomplement of the complex
subspace
T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{t}_{x}X^{\tau}\subseteq T_{x}\tilde{M}
.

We shall refer to
T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau},\,T_{x}^{t}X^{\tau},\,T_{x}^{h}X^{\tau}

as the
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
-vertical,
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
-transverse,
and
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
-horizontal tangent spaces at
x
,
respectively (the terminology being inspired by the
line bundle setting).

We shall accordingly decompose

\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
as

\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}^{t}+\mathbf{v}^{v}+\mathbf{v}^{h},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathbf{v}^{t}\in T_{x}^{t}X^{\tau},\,\mathbf{v}^{v}\in T^{v}_{x}X^{\tau},\,\mathbf{v}^{h}\in T_{x}^{h}X^{\tau}.

Remark 19
.

At any
x\in X^{\tau}
, we have direct sum decompositions

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}=\left(T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{t}_{x}X^{\tau}\right)\oplus T_{x}^{h}X^{\tau},

and

T_{x}Z^{\tau}=\mathcal{T}^{\tau}_{x}\oplus\left(T_{x}^{v}X^{\tau}\oplus T_{x}^{h}X^{\tau}\right),\qquad T_{x}X^{\tau}=T_{x}^{t}X^{\tau}\oplus T_{x}Z^{\tau},

so that
T_{x}^{t}X^{\tau}
is the normal space to
Z^{\tau}

at
x
(in
X^{\tau}
).

Notation 20
.

Given a choice of NHLC’s centered for
X^{\tau}
at
x
, we obtain
a direct sum decomposition of

\mathbb{R}^{2\,d-1}=\mathbb{R}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\cong T_{x}X^{\tau}
, dictated by
\alpha^{\tau}
, where

\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
.
The finer decomposition of
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
in
Definition
18
, dictated by

\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
, determines a
a corresponding decomposition of

\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
, which - emphasizing dimensions -
we shall write in the form

\mathbb{C}^{d-1}=\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{d-1-d_{G}}_{h},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}=J_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}).

We shall shift from real to complex notation,
identifying

\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\cong\mathbb{R}^{2d-2},\quad\mathbb{C}^{d-1-d_{G}}_{h}=\mathbb{R}^{2d-2-2d_{G}}_{h}.

Any
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
may thus be decomposed as

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{v}+\mathbf{v}^{h},\quad\text{where}\quad\mathbf{u}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t},\,\mathbf{u}^{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v},\,\mathbf{u}^{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2-2d_{G}}_{h}.

1.2.1
Scaling asymptotics for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

For the sake of brevity,
we shall first discuss the asymptotic expansions for

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
, and then explain the
necessary changes for
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
.

Before stating our results, we need to make some recalls
and introduce
some further notation.

If
x\in X^{\tau}
, we shall denote by

G_{x}\leqslant G
the stabilizer subgroup
of
x
, and by
r_{x}=|G_{x}|
its cardinality
(
r_{x}
is always finite if
x\in Z^{\tau}
).

Assume as before that Assumption
4
holds
and that
\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
is sufficiently small.
Then for any
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}

the following holds.

1.

There exists a unique
t_{1}=t_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})
such that

x_{1}=\tilde{\mu}_{g}^{\tau}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2})
for some

g\in G
(Lemma
57
below).

2.

There are exactly
r_{x_{1}}

elements
g_{l}\in G

such that
x_{1}=\tilde{\mu}_{g_{l}}^{\tau}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2})

(see Corollary
58
and Remark

59
below).

3.

The condition
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}
is
tantamount to
x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}\neq\emptyset
, and one has

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}=\{x_{12}\},\quad\text{where}\quad x_{12}:=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2}).

(24)

Definition 21
.

Under Assumption
4
,
the
effective volume
at
x\in Z^{\tau}
, denoted

V_{eff}(x)
, is the volume of the
G
-orbit through

x
,
x^{G}\subset X^{\tau}
for the induced
Riemannian density (with respect to
\tilde{\kappa}
- see also
Remark
14
).

Definition 22
.

Assume as above that
\chi
has sufficiently small support,
and that
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}
.
Let
t_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
and
x_{12}\in x_{2}^{\chi}
be
as in (
24
).
Given any choice of NHLC’s at
x_{12}
and
x_{2}
, let
B\in\mathrm{Sp}(2d-2)
be defined by the analogue of
(
23
):

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)=x_{2}+\big(\theta+R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\,B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

(25)

(see Lemma
67
below and the notational remark
preceding Theorem
15
).
Referring to the direct sum decomposition in
Notation
20
,
let us set

\displaystyle A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{v}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2-2d_{G}}_{h}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}

(26)

\displaystyle\left[e^{-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\|B\mathbf{u}\|^{2}}\right].

Definition 23
.

Let
x_{1},\,x_{2}\in X^{\tau}
be as in (
24
),

M
as in (
25
),
and
A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
as in Definition
22
;
define
P
by
(
22
), with
A=B
. We set

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\chi(t_{1})\cdot\frac{\left|\det(P)\right|}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\frac{A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})}{\pi^{d-1}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\dim(\nu)\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(g_{l}\right)},

Remark 24
.

Let us note the following:

1.

Under the previous assumptions,
A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
is finite and non-zero,
and can
in principle be computed in terms of
B
(see Appendix A of
[
F
]
);

2.

if
B
is orthogonal (e.g., if
B
is the
identity matrix), by an interated Gaussian integration one obtains

A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\pi^{d-1}
;

3.

if
B
is orthogonal one also has
|\det(P)|=1
(since in this case
P
is a
unitary complex matrix), hence

\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})=\chi(t_{1})/\big(r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})\big)
.

In view of Theorem
7
,
we expect an exponential decay of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(and
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})
)
along normal displacements to
Z^{\tau}
, that is, when

\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\in T_{x_{j}}^{t}X^{\tau}
.

Theorem 25
.

Suppose that
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})

has sufficiently small support,
and that
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
.
Let
g_{1},\ldots,g_{r_{x_{1}}}\in G
be the distinct elements such
that
x_{1}=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{l}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2})
,
where
t_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
is as in (
24
).
Fix
C>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
.

1.

Uniformly for

\theta_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\in T^{t}_{x_{j}}X^{\tau},\quad|\theta_{j}|,\,\|\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}

we have for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

where for each
l

there is an asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left(\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k,l,\nu}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right];

here

e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\in S^{1}
and

F_{k,l,\nu}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)
is a polynomial in the rescaled variables of
degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
.

2.

Under the same assumptions and with the same notation,
uniformly for

\theta_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\quad|\theta_{j}|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}

\mathbf{v}_{1}=\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}+\mathbf{v}_{2}^{h}\in T^{t}_{x_{1}}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{h}_{x_{1}}X^{\tau},\quad\|\mathbf{v}_{1}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}

\mathbf{v}_{2}=\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\in T^{t}_{x_{2}}X^{\tau},\quad\|\mathbf{v}_{2}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}},

we have for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

where for each
l

there is an asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V},D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}\rangle\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,\tilde{F}_{k,l,\nu}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{V}\right)\right];

here

\tilde{F}_{k,l}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)
is a polynomial in the rescaled variables of
degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
,

\mathbf{V}^{\dagger}:=\begin{pmatrix}(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{\dagger}&(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{h})^{\dagger}&(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t})^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix},

and
D_{l}=D_{l}^{\dagger}
,
\Re(D_{l})\gg 0
for every
l
.

Remark 26
.

The unitary factor
e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)}

is a smooth function on
\mathbb{R}\times X^{\tau}
and is
related to the description (by Zelditch)
of the unitary Toeplitz operators

e^{\imath\,t\,\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}}

as ‘dynamical
Toeplitz operators’ (recall (
10
)); it is therefore an intrinsic invariant, given
a priori
, of
the CR structure of
X^{\tau}
.
Similar considerations apply to the unitary factor

e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t}(x)}
appearing
in the asymptotics of
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
of
§
1.2.2
,
with
e^{\imath\,t\,\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}}

replaced by
U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)

in (
148
) below - see (
149
)
and (
151
) below.

Let us dwell on the special case where

x_{1}=x_{2}=x\in Z^{\tau}\quad\text{and}\quad\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}\big((-\epsilon,\epsilon)\big),

so that

t_{1}=0
and
e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)}=1
.
Furthermore, we fix one system of NHLC’s at
x
, in terms of
which
in (
21
) we write

x_{1,\lambda}:=x+\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{v}_{1}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right),\quad x_{2,\lambda}:=x+\left(\frac{\theta_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{v}_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

(27)

In (
25
) we then have
M=I_{2d-2}
.

Notation 27
.

Suppose
G_{x}=\{\kappa_{l}\}_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}
.
For every
l
,
\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}:T_{x}X^{\tau}\rightarrow T_{x}X^{\tau}
satisfies

\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x)=\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}\left(\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x)\right)\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}\left(\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\right|_{x}\right)=\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\right|_{x}

and
\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}\left(\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}\right)=\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}

(recall (
7
) and
(
20
)).
Thus the action of
\kappa_{l}\in G_{x}
on

T_{x}X^{\tau}=\mathcal{T}^{\tau}_{x}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}\cong\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
has the
form

\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}(\theta,\mathbf{u})=(\theta,\mathbf{u})=\left(\theta,\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}}(\mathbf{u})\right).

In the following, we shall adopt the short-hand

\mathbf{u}^{(l)}:=\mathrm{d}_{x}\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}}(\mathbf{u})\quad\text{for}\quad\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}.

Furthermore, if
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{v}+\mathbf{u}^{h}

then
(\mathbf{u}^{t})^{(l)}=(\mathbf{u}^{(l)})^{t}
,

(\mathbf{u}^{v})^{(l)}=(\mathbf{u}^{(l)})^{v}
,

(\mathbf{u}^{h})^{(l)}=(\mathbf{u}^{(l)})^{h}
.

Theorem 28
.

Under the assumptions and with the notation
of Theorem
25
,
if
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}\big((-\epsilon,\epsilon)\big)

and
x_{1}=x_{2}=x\in Z^{\tau}
, then with the previous notation
the following holds:
uniformly for

\theta_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{h}+\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\in T^{h}_{x_{j}}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{t}_{x_{j}}X^{\tau},\quad|\theta_{j}|,\,\|\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}},

we have for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

where for each
l

there is an asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{r_{x}\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}+\psi_{2}\left((\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\chi(0)\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\right)}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k,l,\nu}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right];

here
F_{k,l,\nu}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)
is a polynomial in the rescaled variables of
degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
.

Theorem
28
is the analogue in the Grauert tube setting
of the equivariant Szegő kernel asymptotics in the line bundle setting of
[
P-2008
]
.

1.2.2
Scaling asymptotics for
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

The scaling asymptotics of
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

can be studied, with some adaptations,
by arguments and techniques similar to
those used for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
.

Theorem 29
.

The statements of Theorems
7
,
15
,

25
and

28

apply with

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
in place of
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
,
with the following changes:

1.

the leading order terms of the asymptotic expansions in
Theorems
25
,
15
and
28

are multiplied by
(\lambda/\tau)^{-(d-1)/2}
;

2.

the real smooth function
\theta^{\tau}_{t}
in the definition of

F_{\chi,\lambda}
is replaced by a possibly different function
real smooth function
\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t}
.

In the action-free case, for example, rather than the expansion
in Theorem
15
we have

\displaystyle P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2\,\pi}\right)^{d-1}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})+\Psi_{M^{-1}}(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right]}\cdot e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x)}\,

\displaystyle\cdot\left[e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\chi(t_{1})+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,\widetilde{F}_{k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)\right],

for certain polynomials
\widetilde{F}_{k}(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot)

in the rescaled variables of
degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
.
The expansion in Theorem 1.9 of
[
CR2
]
follows by rescaling according
to Heisenberg type (as in the Introduction
of
[
P-2024
]
).

1.3
Unrescaled Asymptotics

The equivariant scaling asymptotic expansions of Theorems
25
,

28
,
and
29

hold uniformly, say, on compact subsets
of the locus in
Z^{\tau}
with principal orbit type, but
it is not
a priori
obvious that they hold uniformly near
the locus where the cardinality of the stabilizer has
a discontinuity.
In the following Theorem, which rests on the previous ones,
we establish a near-graph
unrescaled version of the previous
asymptotic expansions; these allow for some
uniform estimates which, in turn, will be useful in the following
applications.

We give an explicit statement and proof in the case of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
; the
extension to
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

can be carried out by the same arguments used for Theorem

29
, and will be left to the reader.

Theorem 30
.

With the previous assumptions and notation, the following holds
for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
.

1.

For small enough

(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}\cong\mathcal{T}^{\tau}_{x_{j}}\oplus T^{t}_{x_{j}}X^{\tau},

there is an asymptotic expansion

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t})\big)\sim\sum_{g_{l}\in G_{x_{1}}}\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t})\big)_{l},

where for each
l

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t})\big)_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left(\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\right)+R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}+\sum_{s\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-s}\,L_{\nu,l,s}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right],

where
R_{3}
vanishes to third order at the origin
and

\Re\left[(\imath\,\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left(\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\right)+R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right]\leq 0.

2.

For small enough

(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\cong T_{x_{j}}X^{\tau}=\mathcal{T}^{\tau}_{x_{j}}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{j}}

with

\mathbf{v}_{1}=\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}+\mathbf{v}_{2}^{h}\in T^{t}_{x_{1}}X^{\tau}\oplus T^{h}_{x_{1}}X^{\tau},\quad\mathbf{v}_{2}=\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\in T^{t}_{x_{2}}X^{\tau},

there is an asymptotic expansion

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\big)\sim\sum_{g_{l}\in G_{x_{1}}}\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\big)_{l},

where for each
l

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\big)_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V},D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}\rangle+R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{V}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}+\sum_{s\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-s}\,K_{\nu,l,s}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{V}\right)\right],

where
\mathbf{V}
is as in Theorem
25
,

R_{3}
vanishes to third order at the origin, and

\Re\left[\imath\,\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V},D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}\rangle+R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{V}\right)\right]\leq 0.

1.4
Applications

We conclude this Introduction with a sample of some of the
possible applications
of the previous asymptotics.

1.4.1
An equivariant global Weyl law for
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

The previous expansions may be integrated to obtain global information on the asymptotic
distribution of the
\lambda_{j}
’s.
Recalling (
15
),
let us set

\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda):=\sum_{\lambda_{j}\leq\lambda}\,\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\qquad(\lambda\in\mathbb{R}).

(28)

We shall make here the simplifying assumption that
G
acts freely on
Z^{\tau}
; then the quotient
Z^{\tau}/G

is a compact manifold; it has a uniquely determined Riemannian structure
such that the projection
Z^{\tau}\rightarrow Z^{\tau}/G
is a Riemannian
submersion. Let
\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)
be the corresponding
volume of
Z^{\tau}/G
.

Theorem 31
.

In the situation of Theorem
28
,
let us assume that
G
acts freely on

Z^{\tau}
.
Then as
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d_{G}}}\cdot\frac{\tau}{d-d_{G}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-d_{G}}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1}\right)\right]

1.4.2
Pointwise estimates on eigenfunctions of

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

Theorem

30
has the following
following straighforward consequence.

Proposition 32
.

There exists

C_{\nu},\,\lambda_{\nu}^{\tau}>0
such that

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,x)\leq C_{\nu}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\qquad\forall\,x\in X^{\tau},\,\lambda\geq\lambda_{\nu}^{\tau}.

Corollary 33
.

There exist
C_{\nu}>0
and
j_{\nu}^{\tau}>0
such that
for any
j\geq j_{\nu}^{\tau}
and
\lambda\geq\lambda_{\nu}^{\tau}

\sum_{k}|\rho_{j,\nu,k}(x)|^{2}\leq C_{\nu}\,\left(\frac{\lambda_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\qquad(x\in X^{\tau}).

1.4.3
Operator norm estimates
for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

In the action-free case, Chang and Rabinowitz have
established in
[
CR2
]

operator norm estimates on

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}:L^{p}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow L^{q}(X^{\tau})
.
Namely, they proved that
for
2\leq p,q\leq+\infty
there
exist constants
C^{\tau}_{p}>0
such that

\left\|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}\rightarrow L^{q}}\leq C^{\tau}_{p}\,\lambda^{(d-1)\,\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}\right)}.

(29)

This result is the analogue of similar estimates proved in the
line bundle setting by Shiffman and Zelditch in
[
SZ03
]
,
and the proof follows the same general lines, adapted to the
Grauert tube context.
More precisely, the argument given
by Chang and Rabinowitz
for (
29
)
is based on the off-graph scaling asymptotics of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}
, paired with the Shur-Young inequality

[
So
]
.

The goal of this section is to describe
an equivariant extension of (
29
),
involving the operator norm of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}:L^{p}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow L^{q}(X^{\tau})
.

Theorem
30

allows an adaptation to the equivariant setting of the arguments in
[
CR2
]
for the
proof of (
29
).

Theorem 34
.

Under the previous assumptions, there exists a constant

\rho^{\tau}_{p,\nu}>0
such that

\left\|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}\rightarrow L^{q}}\leq\rho_{p,\nu}^{\tau}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}\right)\,\left(d-1-\frac{d_{G}}{2}\right)}.

1.4.4
Estimates on the complexifications

\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}
’s.

We discuss some estimates on equivariant complexified
eigenfunctions of
\Delta
, which are
equivariant analogues of estimates in

[
Z-2020
]
. The proofs are either straightforward, or
minor modifications of the ones for the eigenfunctions
of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
.

Let
\chi
be as in §
1.4.2
;
Then
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,x)\geq 0
.
Furthermore, as we have remarked an analogue of Theorem

30
holds for
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
,
leading to the following analogue of Proposition
32
:

Proposition 35
.

For some constant
C_{\nu}>0
and every

x\in X^{\tau}
and
\lambda\gg 0
we have

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(x,x\right)\leq C_{\nu}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{(d-1-d_{G})/2}.

With
\lambda=\mu_{j}
we deduce the following.

Corollary 36
.

If
x\in X^{\tau}
and
j\gg 0
,

\sum_{k}\left|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(x)\right|^{2}\leq C_{\nu}\,e^{2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\left(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\tau}\right)^{(d-1-d_{G})/2}.

(30)

As in
[
Z-2020
]
,
[
CR1
]
,
[
CR2
]
, let us consider
the (equivariant) Husimi distributions

U_{j,\nu,k}(x):=\dfrac{\left|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(x)\right|^{2}}{\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2}}.

(31)

By Lemma 0.2 of
[
Z-2020
]
,

\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2}\sim D_{\tau}\,e^{2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\mu_{j}^{-(d-1)/2}

for some constant
D_{\tau}>0
.
We obtain the following equivariant
refinement of the action-free upper bound in
[
Z-2020
]
.

Corollary 37
.

Under the previous assumptions, for
j\gg 0
and every
k

\sup_{x\in X^{\tau}}U_{j,\nu,k}(x)\leq D_{\tau}^{\prime\prime}\,\mu_{j}^{d-1-\frac{1}{2}\,d_{G}}.

We conclude with an
L^{2}
-analogue of the Weyl law for

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
in Theorem
31
.

Proposition 38
.

Under the assumptions and with the notation
of Theorem
31
,
the following asymptotic expansion holds for

\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{trace}\left(P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\mu_{j})\,e^{-2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k}\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\cdot\pi^{d_{G}}}{(2\,\pi)^{d}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}-d_{G}}\,\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k}\,F_{k,\nu}\left(x\right)\right]

Setting
\lambda=\mu_{j}
, we obtain the following consequence.

Corollary 39
.

There is a constanct
C_{\nu}^{\tau}>0

such that for all
j\gg 0

\sum_{k}\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2}\leq C_{\nu}^{\tau}\,e^{2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\mu_{j}^{\frac{d-1}{2}-d_{G}}.

The asymptotic expansion in Proposition
38
may be translsated into
information about the asymptotics of

\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{\nu}(\lambda)=\sum_{\mu_{j}\leq\lambda}e^{-2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k}\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2},

(32)

essentially by the same
argument used for Theorem
31
.

Theorem 40
.

In the situation of Theorem
31
, let us also assume
that
d\geq 2\,d_{G}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{d_{G}}}{(2\,\pi)^{d}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}-d_{G}}\,\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\dfrac{\lambda}{\frac{d+1}{2}-d_{G}}+O(1)\right].

2
Preliminaries

We shall identify the tangent and cotangent bundles of
M
,

TM
and
T^{\vee}M
, by means of
\kappa
.

2.1
The action on the Grauert tubes

In Section

1
, the Grauert tubes

\tilde{M}^{\tau}
,
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
have been described
as strictly pseudoconvex domains in
\tilde{M}
intrisically associated
to
\kappa
.
An alternative characterization of
\tilde{M}^{\tau}

is in terms of disk bundles

T^{\tau}M\subset T^{\vee}M
of norm
\tau
with respect to

\kappa
, where
T^{\vee}M
has been endowed
in a neighbourhood of the zero section
M_{0}
with a
complex structure intrinsically associated to
\kappa

(
[
LS
]
,
[
Lem
]
,
[
Sz
]
,
[
GS-1991
]
,
[
GS-1992
]
).
This complex structure was called
adapted
in
[
LS
]
,
and will be denoted
J_{ad}
; it
is uniquely determined by the condition that
the Riemannian and Monge-Ampère foliations coincide

[
LS
]
.

Notation 41
.

Let
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow M
be a geodesic, with
velocity curve
\dot{\gamma}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow TM
.
Let us define
\psi_{\gamma}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow TM

by

\psi_{\gamma}:a+\imath\,b\mapsto N_{b}\big(\dot{\gamma}(a)\big),

where
N_{b}
denotes dilation by the factor
b
.

Theorem 42
.

(Lempert and Szöke)
Let
J_{0}
be the complex structure on
\mathbb{C}
.
Given any sufficiently small neighbourhood
T^{\prime}M\subseteq TM

of the zero section,

J_{ad}
is the unique complex structure on

T^{\prime}M\subseteq TM
such that

\psi_{\gamma}:\psi_{\gamma}^{-1}(T^{\prime}M)\rightarrow T^{\prime}M
is

(J_{0},J_{ad})
-holomorphic for every geodesic
\gamma
on
(M,\kappa)
.

As proved in
[
LS
]
and
[
GLS
]
,
for some tubular neighbourhood
T^{\prime}M\subseteq T^{\vee}M

of the zero section
the

imaginary time exponential map

E:T^{\prime}M\rightarrow\tilde{M}

(essentially defined by
complexifying the ordinary exponential map)
is
(J_{ad},J)
-holomorphic and
intertwines the square norm function with
\rho
and
the canonical symplectic structure
\Omega_{can}
on
T^{\vee}M

with
\Omega
(we conform to the notation and conventions in

[
P-2024
]
, thus
\Omega_{can}=\mathrm{d}\mathbf{q}\wedge\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}
in local
(\mathbf{q},\mathbf{p})
coordinates).
Hence
E
restricts to an isomorphism of Kähler manifolds

E^{\tau}:(T^{\tau}M,\Omega_{can},J)\cong(\tilde{M}^{\tau},\Omega,J).

Notation 43
.

Given
f:M\rightarrow M
smooth,

\mathrm{d}f:TM\rightarrow TM
will denote its
differential (tangent map).

Proposition 44
.

If
f:M\rightarrow M
is a Riemannian isometry of
(M,\kappa)
, then

\mathrm{d}f
restricts to a biholomorphism of
(T^{\tau}M,J_{\mathrm{ad}})
into itself, for any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
.

Proof.

The claim is that

\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)\circ J_{\mathrm{ad}}=J_{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)

(33)

on
T(T^{\tau}M)
. Let
(m,v)\in TM\setminus M_{0}
, and let
\gamma:\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow M
be the unique geodesic
with
\dot{\gamma}(0)=(m,v)
.
Since
f
is an isometry, the geodesic corresponding to

\mathrm{d}f(m,v)
is
f\circ\gamma
, and its velocity is

\mathrm{d}f(\dot{\gamma})
. Hence,

\mathrm{d}f\circ\psi_{\gamma}(a+\imath\,b)=\mathrm{d}f\circ N_{b}\left(\dot{\gamma}(a)\right)=N_{b}\circ\mathrm{d}f\left(\dot{\gamma}(a)\right)=N_{b}\circ\dot{\overbrace{{f\circ\gamma}}}(a)=\psi_{f\circ\gamma}(a+\imath\,b).

Thus, by Theorem
42

\mathrm{d}f\circ\psi_{\gamma}=\psi_{f\circ\gamma}

is
(J_{0},J_{\mathrm{ad}})
-holomorphic for every
\gamma

(on the preimage in
\mathbb{C}
of
T^{\tau}M
).
In other worlds,

J_{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f\circ\psi_{\gamma})=\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f\circ\psi_{\gamma})\circ J_{0}.

By the chain rule,

\left(\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)^{-1}\circ J_{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)\right)\circ\mathrm{d}\psi_{\gamma}=\mathrm{d}\psi_{\gamma}\circ J_{0}.

By the uniqueness part in Theorem
42
,

\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)^{-1}\circ J_{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}f)=J_{0},

i.e. (
33
) holds.
∎

Corollary 45
.

Any isometry of
(M,\kappa)
is real-analytic.

Therefore, if
f
is an isometry of
(M,\kappa)
, it uniquely
extends
to a holomorphic map
\tilde{f}:\tilde{M}^{\tau}\rightarrow\tilde{M}

for any sufficiently small
\tau>0
.

Corollary 46
.

For any
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
the following holds:

1.

\tilde{f}
is defined on
\tilde{M}^{\tau}
,
where it is the biholomorphism

\tilde{f}=E^{\tau}\circ\mathrm{d}f\circ(E^{\tau})^{-1}:\tilde{M}^{\tau}\rightarrow\tilde{M}^{\tau};

2.

\tilde{f}\circ\rho=\rho
,
\tilde{f}^{*}(\Omega)=\Omega
;

3.

\tilde{f}
restricts to a CR and contact automorphism

\tilde{f}^{\tau}:X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}
.

Applying the above to a smooth action by isometries, we conclude the following.

Proposition 47
.

Let
(M,\kappa)
be a compact
\mathcal{C}^{\varpi}
Riemannian manifold,
G
a compact Lie group, and

\mu:G\times M\rightarrow M
a smooth action as a group of isometries of
(M,\kappa)
. Then:

1.

for every
g\in G
,
\mu_{g}:M\rightarrow M
is real-analytic;

2.

for
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
\mu
extends to a
G
-action
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}:G\times\tilde{M}^{\tau}\rightarrow\tilde{M}^{\tau}
as a group of automorphisms of the Kähler manifold

(\tilde{M}^{\tau},\Omega,J)
;

3.

E^{\tau}
intertwines
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
on
\tilde{M}^{\tau}
with
the (co)tangent action on
T^{\tau}M
;

4.

(\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}_{g})^{*}(\alpha)=\alpha,\,\forall\,g\in G
;

5.

\tilde{\mu}
restricts to an action
\mu^{\tau}:G\times X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}
as a group of automorphisms of the contact CR manifold
X^{\tau}
.

Proof.

The only thing left to prove is that
\tilde{\mu}_{g}^{*}(\alpha^{\tau})=\alpha^{\tau}
for all
g\in G
.
Since
\tilde{\mu}_{g}
is holomorphic, preserves
\rho
and
\alpha=\Im(\partial\rho)
, the thesis follows by restriction to
X^{\tau}
.
∎

The (co)tangent lift of
\mu
preserves
the tautological 1-form

\lambda_{\mathrm{\mathrm{can}}}
on
T^{\vee}M

(locally given by
\mathbf{p}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{q}
),
and is therefore Hamiltonian with respect to
\Omega_{\mathrm{\mathrm{can}}}
, with moment map

\Phi^{\xi}_{TM}=\langle\Phi_{TM},\xi\rangle=\lambda_{\mathrm{\mathrm{can}}}(\xi_{TM}).

On the other hand, for every
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})

\alpha=-{E^{\tau}}^{*}(\lambda_{can})\quad\text{on}\quad M^{\tau}

(see the discussion in the introduction of
[
P-2024
]
).

Given
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
and
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})
,
we shall denote by

\xi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}}
and
\xi_{X^{\tau}}
the induced vector
fields on
\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}
and
X^{\tau}
, respectively.
Hence
\xi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}}

is tangent to
X^{\tau}
and
\xi_{X^{\tau}}
is the restriction of

\xi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}}
to
X^{\tau}
.

By the previous discussion (see also Lemma 22 of
[
P-2024
]
),
we conclude the following.

Corollary 48
.

With the preceding assumptions and notation,
for every
\tau\in(0,\tau_{0})

the following holds.

1.

\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
is Hamiltonian for
\Omega
,
with moment map
\Phi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau}}:\tilde{M}^{\tau}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}

given by

\varphi^{\xi}:=\langle\Phi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau}},\xi\rangle=-\alpha(\xi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau}})\quad(\xi\in\mathfrak{g}),

2.

For any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
, we have

\xi_{X^{\tau}}=\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}-\varphi^{\xi}\,\mathcal{R}^{\tau}
,
where
\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}
is a smooth section of
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}

and we write
\varphi^{\xi}
for
\left.\varphi^{\xi}\right|_{X^{\tau}}
.

Notation 49
.

We stress the notation used above:
\tilde{\mu}^{\tau}
is the
action of
G
on
\tilde{M}^{\tau}
as a group of Kähler automorphisms;

\mu^{\tau}
is the restricted action of
G

on
X^{\tau}
by contact CR automorphisms.
Furthermore, in the following we shall generally simplify notation
and write
\Phi
for
\Phi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau}}
.

Since
\rho
is
G
-invariant,

[\xi_{\tilde{M}^{\tau_{0}}},\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}]=0
, hence

\Phi
is constant along the geodesic flow.
Recalling (
7
) and (
19
),
we conclude the following.

Corollary 50
.

\mathcal{R}^{\tau}
is tangent to
Z^{\tau}
.

2.2
\Pi^{\tau}
in NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}

As mentioned in §
1
,
our computations will be carried out in so-called
normal Heisenberg
local coordinates
on
X^{\tau}

(recall the discussion surrounding (
20
) and
(
21
)); these are induced
(by projection and restriction)
by suitable holomorphic local coordinates on
\tilde{M}
, in which
the defining equation of
X^{\tau}
has a canonical approximate form,
and which are also called NHLC’s (on
\tilde{M}
).

NHLC’s were defined in §3.3 of
[
P-2024
]
, and
are a slight specialization of the normal coordinates introduced in

[
FS1
]
and
[
FS2
]
, and first put to use
(under the name of Heisenberg local coordinates) in the present setting in

[
CR1
]
and
[
CR2
]
. In the line bundle context, Heisenberg local
coordinates have been thoroughly
used in the study of scaling asymptotics since their appearance in
[
SZ02
]
.

In NHLC’s on
\tilde{M}
centered at
x\in X^{\tau}
,
\alpha
,
\Omega
and
\hat{\kappa}
(and hence also
\omega=\frac{1}{2}\Omega
and
\tilde{\kappa}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{\kappa}
) have a simple local approximate expression, for which we refer to Proposition 34
of
[
P-2024
]
. Another key point in our discussion is that the phase and the
leading order term of the symbol of the Szegő kernel
\Pi^{\tau}

can be computed fairly explicitly in NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
.

As proved in
[
BdM-S
]
,
\Pi^{\tau}
in
(
9
)
is a Fourier integral operator with complex phase.
Its wave front is the anti-diagonal
(\Sigma^{\tau})^{\sharp}
of

\Sigma^{\tau}
in (
6
):

(\Sigma^{\tau})^{\sharp}:=\left\{\left(x,r\alpha^{\tau}_{x},x,-r\alpha^{\tau}_{x}\right)\ \big|\ x\in X^{\tau},r>0\right\}\subseteq T^{\lor}X^{\tau}\setminus(0)\times T^{\lor}X^{\tau}\setminus(0).

Up to a smoothing kernel, the distributional kernel
\Pi^{\tau}(\cdot,\cdot)

has the form:

\Pi^{\tau}(x,y)\sim\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\imath u\psi^{\tau}(x,y)}s^{\tau}(x,y,u)\mathrm{d}u,

(34)

where

1.

s^{\tau}
is a classical symbol of type:

s^{\tau}(x,y,u)\sim\sum_{j\geq 0}u^{d-1-j}s_{j}^{\tau}(x,y);

2.

\psi^{\tau}
is a phase of positive type and is determined by:

\psi^{\tau}:=-\imath\tilde{\phi}^{\tau}\big|_{X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}}

where
\tilde{\phi}
is the holomorphic extension to
\tilde{M}\times\overline{\tilde{M}}
of the defining function
\phi^{\tau}=\rho-\tau^{2}
of
X^{\tau}
in
\tilde{M}

(see the discussions in
[
CR1
]
,
[
CR2
]
, and
§3.3.2 of
[
P-2024
]
- and of course
[
BdM-S
]
).

For the following, see Proposition 48 and Theorem 51 of
[
P-2024
]
.

Theorem 51
.

Suppose
x\in X^{\tau}
and fix a NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}

centered at
x

and defined on an open neighourhood
U^{\tau}\subseteq X^{\tau}

of
x
. Then, if
x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in U^{\tau}
are of the form
x^{\prime}=x+(\theta,z^{\prime})
,
x^{\prime\prime}=x+(\eta,z^{\prime\prime})
, we have:

\imath\psi^{\tau}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})=\imath(\theta-\eta)-\frac{1}{4\tau^{2}}(\theta-\eta)^{2}+\psi^{\omega_{x}}_{2}(z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime})+R_{3}(\theta,z^{\prime},\overline{z^{\prime}},\eta,z^{\prime\prime},\overline{z^{\prime\prime}}).

Moreover, in the same chart, the principal term of the symbol along the diagonal takes the form

s^{\tau}_{0}(x,x)=\frac{\tau}{(2\pi)^{d}}.

2.3
The submanifold
Z^{\tau}

Our analysis rests on the assumption that
G
acts locally freely on

Z^{\tau}
in (
19
). This hypothesis is certainly
satisfied if
\mu
itself is locally free - for instance if

M
is the total space of a principal
G
-bundle.
This is not the only circumstance.
Here we give two sufficient conditions,
whose proof is left to the reader.

Lemma 52
.

With the previous assumptions and notation,

\mu^{\tau}
is locally free on
Z^{\tau}
under the following
two sets of circumstances.

1.

\mu
is locally free away from a finite set of
fixed points.

2.

G
is Abelian, and if
T_{1},\ldots,T_{r}\leqslant G

are the subtori that appear as stabilizers of poits in
M

then
the connected components of the fixed locus of each
T_{i}
in
M

are single
G
-orbits of
\mu
.

We record a statement in the opposite direction,
whose proof is also left to the reader.

Lemma 53
.

Suppose that there exists a submanifold
N\subseteq M
of positive dimension such that

\mu_{g}(n)=n
for every
g\in G
and
n\in N
.
Then
\mu^{\tau}
is not locally free
on
Z^{\tau}
.

If
G
acts locally freely on
Z\setminus M
, the
latter is a submanifold of
\tilde{M}
of codimension
d_{G}
.
Given that
Z
is obviously transverse to
X^{\tau}
,
we conclude the following.

Lemma 54
.

Under Assumption
4
,

Z^{\tau}
is a submanifold of
X^{\tau}
of dimension
2d-1-d_{G}
.

Since
G
acts locally freely on
Z^{\tau}
,

d_{G}\leq\mathrm{dim}(Z^{\tau})
.

Corollary 55
.

d_{G}\leq d-1
.

2.4
Intersections of
G
-orbits and
\mathbb{R}
-orbits

The orbits of the the geodesic flow

t\mapsto\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}
on
X^{\tau}
are ‘vertical’
(i.e., tangent to

\mathcal{T}^{\tau}
in (
8
));
in view of (
48
),
along
Z^{\tau}
the orbits of the
G
-action
g\mapsto\mu^{\tau}_{g}
are ‘horizontal’  (i.e., tangent to
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
).
Since under Assumption
4

both actions are locally free on
Z^{\tau}
and commute,
the product action of

G\times\mathbb{R}
is also locally free on
Z^{\tau}
.

Points
x_{1},\,x_{2}\in Z^{\tau}
belong to the same
G\times\mathbb{R}

orbit, that is,
x_{1}\in x_{2}^{G\times\mathbb{R}}
, if and only if the

\mathbb{R}
-orbit of either one intersects the
G
-orbit of the other,
i.e.

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\mathbb{R}}\neq\emptyset
, and then the intersection might be infinite. However, given a
compact subset
K\subset\mathbb{R}
by the above there are
at most finitely many
t\in K
for which
\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})\in x_{1}^{G}
. A uniform statement can be given in terms
of the size of
K
, but first we need to lay down some useful
consequences of the local freeness of the action of
G\times\mathbb{R}

on the compact manifold
Z^{\tau}
; the proof is left to the reader.

Lemma 56
.

Under Assumption
4
, the following holds.

1.

There exists
R,\,r>0
such that if

(g,t)
belongs to an
R
-neighbourhood of
(e_{G},0)
then

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,\mu^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y)\right)\geq r\,\mathrm{dist}_{G\times\mathbb{R}}\big((g,t),(e_{G},0)\big).

2.

There exist
C>0
such that

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x,\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)<C\,|t|,\quad\forall\,(x,t)\in Z^{\tau}\times\mathbb{R}.

3.

There exists
D>0
such that
for any
y\in Z^{\tau}
and
\delta>0
sufficiently small

\mathrm{dist}_{G}(g,G_{y})\geq\delta\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,\mu^{\tau}_{g}(y)\right)\geq\frac{\delta}{D}.

Lemma 57
.

Assume that

\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small and

\chi\in\mathrm{C}^{\infty}_{c}\big((t_{0}-\epsilon,t_{0}+\epsilon)\big)

for some
t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then for any
x\in Z^{\tau}
and
x^{\prime}\in x^{G\times\chi}
there exists a unique

t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
such that

x^{\prime}=\mu^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)

for some
g\in G
.

Proof of Lemma
57
.

If the Lemma is false, for any
j=1,2,\ldots
there exist

x_{j},\,x_{j}^{\prime}\in Z^{\tau}
,

t_{j},\,t_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
g_{j},\,g_{j}^{\prime}\in G
such that
such that

0<|t_{j}-t_{j}^{\prime}|<\frac{1}{j}\quad\text{and}\quad x_{j}^{\prime}=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{\prime}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}}(x_{j})=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}}(x_{j}).

Hence

x_{j}=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x_{j}).

(35)

By statement 2 of Lemma
56
,

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x,\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x)\right)<C/j\quad\forall\,x\in X^{\tau}.

By the triangle inequality, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{j},\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}}(x_{j})\right)

(36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{j},\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x_{j})\right)+\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x_{j}),\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}}(x_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0+C/j=C/j.

Hence, by statement 3 of Lemma
56
,

\mathrm{dist}_{G}\left(g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime},G_{x_{j}}\right)\leq(C\cdot D)/j.

We may thus find
\sigma_{j}\in G_{x_{j}}
and
\delta_{j}\in G
such that

g_{j}^{-1}\,g_{j}^{\prime}=\delta_{j}\,\sigma_{j},\quad\mathrm{dist}_{G}\left(\delta_{j},e_{G}\right)\leq D/j.

Then (
35
) implies

x_{j}=\mu^{\tau}_{\delta_{j}\,\sigma_{j}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x_{j})=\mu^{\tau}_{\delta_{j}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j}}(x_{j}),

(37)

and
(\delta_{j},t_{j}^{\prime}-t_{j})
belongs to a neighbourhood of
(0,e_{G})

of radius
O\left(1/j\right)
.
By statement 1 of Lemma
56

this is absurd, unless
\delta_{j}=e_{G}
and
t_{j}=t_{j}^{\prime}
.

∎

Corollary 58
.

If
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small and

|\mathrm{supp}(\chi)|<2\,\epsilon
,
then for any
x_{1},\,x_{2}\in Z^{\tau}
we have the following alternative;
either
x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}=\emptyset
, or else

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}=\{x_{12}\}
for a unique
x_{12}\in Z^{\tau}
.

Remark 59
.

In the assumptions of Corollary
58
,
suppose that
x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}\neq\emptyset
;
equivalently,

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2}):=\left\{(g,t)\in G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)\,:\,x_{1}=\mu^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})\right\}\neq\emptyset.

(38)

Let
t_{1}=t_{1}(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
be the unique element
whose existence is asserted in Lemma
57
,
and choose

h_{1}\in G
such that
(h_{1},t_{1})\in\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
.
Let
G_{x_{1}}:=\left\{\kappa_{l}\,:\,l=1,\ldots,r_{x_{1}}\right\}\leqslant G
denote the stabilizer subgroup of
x_{1}
. Then

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\left\{(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1},t_{1})\,:\,l=1,\ldots,r_{x_{1}}\right\}.

(39)

2.5
The action in Heisenberg local coordinates

As is the Introduction (recall the discussion surrounding
(
21
)), NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered
at
x
will be denoted
in additive notation:

y(\theta,\mathbf{v})=x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})
.
Given
x\in X^{\tau}
, we can find an open neighbourhood

U^{\tau}\subseteq X^{\tau}
of
x
and
a smoothly varying family of systems of NHLC’s

y_{x^{\prime}}(\theta,\mathbf{v})=x^{\prime}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})

centered at
x^{\prime}\in X^{\tau}
.
More explicitly, for some
\delta>0

we have a smooth map

\Upsilon:U^{\tau}\times(-\delta,\delta)\times B_{2d-2}(\mathbf{0},\delta)\rightarrow X^{\tau}

such that for every
x^{\prime}\in U^{\tau}

the partial function
\Upsilon(x^{\prime},\cdot,\cdot)
is a system of NHLC’s
centered at
x^{\prime}
. Hence

y_{x^{\prime}}(\theta,\mathbf{v})=\Upsilon(x^{\prime},\theta,\mathbf{u})=x^{\prime}+(\theta,\mathbf{u}).

We refer to the notation and conventions in
[
P-2024
]

(see also
[
CR1
]
and
[
CR2
]
).
In this section, we shall adapt some arguments
from
[
P2-2012
]
to express the action
of
G\times\mathbb{R}
on
X^{\tau}
in terms of
NHLC’s.

Let us first consider the geodesic flow.
The following is a consequence of (105) of
[
P-2024
]
.

Lemma 60
.

Suppose
x\in X^{\tau}
, and let us choose Heisenberg local coordinates on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x
. If
y=x+(\theta,\mathbf{u})
,
then

\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y)=x+\Big(\theta-\tau\,t+R_{3}(\tau,t,\theta,\mathbf{u}),\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\tau\,t,\theta,\mathbf{u})\Big).

The convention for the meaning of
R_{k}
and
\mathbf{R}_{k}

is as explained in the discussion of (
23
).

To proceed, we need to relate systems of NHLC’s centered at nearby
points. We shall adapt an argument in
[
P2-2012
]
to the present setting.

Lemma 61
.

For
(\theta_{,}\mathbf{u}_{j})\sim(0,\mathbf{0})
,

\displaystyle x+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{u}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(x+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1})\big)

\displaystyle+\Big(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}+\omega(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})+R_{3}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j}),\mathbf{u}_{2}-\mathbf{u}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})\Big).

Proof.

We can write

x+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{u}_{2})=\big(x+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1})\big)+\big(\beta(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j}),\mathbf{B}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})\big)

for certain smooth functions
\beta
(real-valued) and

\mathbf{B}
(vector-valued), vanishing at the origin.
Let us expand
\beta
and
\mathbf{B}
:

\beta=\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}+R_{3},\quad\mathbf{B}=\mathbf{B}_{1}+\mathbf{R}_{2},

where
\beta_{1}
and
\mathbf{B}_{1}
are linear, and
\beta_{2}
is homogeneous of degree
2
.
It is easily seen that

\beta_{1}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})=\theta_{2}-\theta_{1},\quad\mathbf{B}_{1}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})=\mathbf{u}_{2}-\mathbf{u}_{1};

(40)

let us then determine
\beta_{2}
.

Let us choose
\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{0}\big([-\epsilon,\epsilon]\big)

for some sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. Keeping

\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j}
fixed, for
\lambda>0

let
x_{j,\lambda}
be as in (
27
)
and apply Theorem 1 in
[
P-2024
]
.

On the one hand,
for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
we have

\displaystyle\Pi_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(0)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})+\psi_{2}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})\right]}\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\right)\right].

On the other hand,
by
(
40
) we have

\displaystyle x_{2,\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{1,\lambda}+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\left(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\beta_{2}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})+\sqrt{\lambda}\,\beta_{3}\left(\frac{\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\,\frac{\mathbf{u}_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\right),\right.

(42)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,(\mathbf{u}_{2}-\mathbf{u}_{1})+\mathbf{R}_{3}\left(\frac{\theta_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\right)

Hence, again by Theorem 1 in
[
P-2024
]
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\chi(0)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\imath\,\beta_{2}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{u}_{1}-\mathbf{u}_{2}\|^{2}\right]}\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\right)\right].

Thus

-\imath\,\beta_{2}(\theta_{j},\mathbf{u}_{j})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{u}_{1}-\mathbf{u}_{2}\|^{2}=\psi_{2}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})=-\imath\,\omega_{x}(\mathbf{u}_{1},\mathbf{u}_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{u}_{1}-\mathbf{u}_{2}\|^{2}.

The claim follows.
∎

Corollary 62
.

Under the same assumptions,

\displaystyle\Big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{u})\Big)+\big(\beta,\mathbf{B}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+\beta-\omega_{x}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{B})+R_{3},\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big),

where
R_{3}=R_{3}(\theta,\beta,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{B})
,
\mathbf{R}_{2}=\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\beta,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{B})
.

Let us now express the
G
-action near a point
x\in X^{\tau}
in NHLC’s.
We consider both the case of a fixed
g\in G_{x}
(the stabilizer subgroup of
x
), and that of a 1-parameter
subgroup of
G
generated by a given
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Let us premise a remark. If
g\in G_{x}
, then

\mathrm{d}_{x}\mu^{\tau}_{g}:T_{x}X^{\tau}\rightarrow T_{x}X^{\tau}
preserves both the
vertical and horizontal tangent vector bundles at
x
; furthermore,
