\mathrm{d}_{x}\mu^{\tau}_{g}\big(\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x)\big)=\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x)
.
If
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{\tau}(x)
, we shall simplify notation and write

\mathbf{v}_{g}:=\mathrm{d}_{x}\mu^{\tau}_{g}\big(\mathbf{v}\big).

(44)

Lemma 63
.

Suppose
x\in X^{\tau}
and choose a system of NHLC’s centered at
x
.
Then the following holds.

1.

If
\kappa\in G_{x}
,

\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa^{-1}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)=x+\Big(\theta+R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}_{\kappa^{-1}}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\Big).

2.

If
\xi\sim 0\in\mathfrak{g}
and

(\theta,\mathbf{v})\sim(0,\mathbf{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
,
then

\mu^{\tau}_{e^{-\xi}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)=x+\Big(\theta+\varphi^{\xi}(x)+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big),

where

R_{3}=R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v},\xi)
,

\mathbf{R}_{2}=\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v},\xi)
.

Remark 64
.

If
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{\tau}(x)
, then

\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)=\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)
.

Proof of 1.

Composing the given system of NHLC’s with
\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa^{-1}}
yields another system of
NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x
. Hence, by the discussion in
§3.3.5 of
[
P-2024
]
, the angular coordinates of the two systems only
differ to third order.
∎

Proof of 2.

With the notation of Corollary
48
,
since
\mu^{\tau}
and the geodesic flow commute

\left[\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}},\varphi^{\xi}\,\mathcal{R}^{\tau}\right]=\left[\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}},\mathcal{R}^{\tau}\right]=0.

Let us denote by
\Upsilon_{t,V}
the flow at time
t
of
a smooth vector field

V
on
X^{\tau}
.
Then, if
\mu^{\tau}_{t,\xi}:=\Upsilon_{t,\xi_{X^{\tau}}}
, for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
:

\mu^{\tau}_{-t,\xi}=\Upsilon_{t,\varphi^{\xi}\,\mathcal{R}}\circ\Upsilon_{-t,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}}:X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}.

For any
x^{\prime}\in X^{\tau}
, the curve
t\mapsto\Upsilon_{-t,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}}(x)

is horizontal, since
\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}
is tangent to

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}
(here ‘horizontal’  is the sense
of (
8
)).
By §3.3.7 of
[
P-2024
]
,

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{-t,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)+\Big(R_{3}(t),-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t)\Big).

In view of Lemma
61
, this may be rewritten

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{-t,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+t\,\omega_{x}(\mathbf{v},\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x))+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big),

where we abridge
R_{3}=R_{3}(\theta,t,\mathbf{v})
,
and similarly for
\mathbf{R}_{2}
.
Then by Lemma
60

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{-t,\xi}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

(45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{t,\varphi^{\xi}\,\mathcal{R}}\Big(x+\Big(\theta+t\,\omega_{x}(\mathbf{v},\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x))+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+t\,\omega_{x}(\mathbf{v},\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x))+t\,\varphi^{\xi}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+t\,\omega_{x}(\mathbf{v},\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x))+t\,\varphi^{\xi}(x)+2\,t\,\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp},\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+t\,\varphi^{\xi}(x)+t\,\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp},\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-t\,\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big).

∎

Corollary 65
.

In the situation and with the notation
of Lemma
63
,
if in addition
x\in Z^{\tau}
then

\mu^{\tau}_{e^{-\xi}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)=x+\Big(\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big).

We shall need to localize our computations in
X^{\tau}

near points

x\in Z^{\tau}
and in
G
near
G_{x}
.
Let
G_{x}=\{\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{r}\}
, and for each
l=1,\ldots,r

let us parametrize
G
in the neighbourhood of
\kappa_{l}
by setting

g=e^{\xi}\,\kappa_{l},\quad\text{where}\quad\xi\sim 0\in\mathfrak{g}.

(46)

Furthermore, we shall further abridge notation (
44
),
and set

\mathbf{v}^{(l)}:=\mathbf{v}_{k_{l}^{-1}}.

(47)

Then, by Corollary
65
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{e^{-\xi}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

(48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}}\left(x+\Big(\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big)\right).

Applying 1. of Lemma
63
, we obtain
(with
g
as in (
46
))

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}\big(x+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

(49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x+\Big(\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}^{(l)}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)}+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big).

Remark 66
.

For any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
and
l=1,\ldots,r
,

\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)}=\mathrm{Ad}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}}(\xi)_{X^{\tau}}(x).

2.6
\psi^{\tau}
near orbit intersections

Let us now assume that
x_{1},x_{2}\in Z^{\tau}
are such that

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}\neq\emptyset
, i.e.

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})\neq\emptyset
(recall (
38
)).
Thus, assuming
|\mathrm{supp}(\chi)|
is sufficiently small,

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
is as in (
39
).
By Corollary
58
,

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\chi}=\{x_{12}\}
, where

x_{12}:=\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}(x_{1})=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{2}).

We shall need to expand the phase
\psi^{\tau}

in (
34
) near

(x_{12},x_{12})
and
(x_{2},x_{2})
.
We assume given systems of NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered at

x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.
The choice of
h_{1}
then uniquely determines
NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x_{12}
, by
the condition that
\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}
be locally represented by
the identity from a neighbourhood of
x_{1}
to one of
x_{12}
.

2.6.1
\psi^{\tau}
near
(x_{12},x_{12})

In the neighbourhood of
\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}

(recall (
39
)),
we can parameterize
G
by setting

g:=e^{\xi}\,\kappa_{l}\,h_{1},\quad\text{where}\quad\xi\sim 0\in\mathfrak{g}.

Making use of (
49
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})\big)=\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}\,e^{-\xi}}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})\big)

(50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}\left(x_{1}+\Big(\theta_{1}+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(l)}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)}+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{12}+\Big(\theta_{1}+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)}+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big).

In the neighbourhood of
(x_{12},x_{12})
,
we shall consider pairs of the form

\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\big),x_{12}+\big(\theta,\mathbf{v}\big)\right),\quad g=e^{\xi}\,\kappa_{l}\,h_{1};

the first entry is given by (
50
).
By the discussion in §
2.2

(see also Proposition 47 of
[
P-2024
]
), we then have:

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}\big(x_{1}+(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})\big),x_{12}+\big(\theta,\mathbf{v}\big)\right)

(51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(x_{12}+\Big(\theta_{1}+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+R_{3},\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)}+\mathbf{R}_{2}\Big),x_{12}+\big(\theta,\mathbf{v}\big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\Big(\theta_{1}-\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)\Big)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,\Big(\theta_{1}-\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)},\mathbf{v}\right)+R_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\Big(\theta_{1}-\theta+\omega_{x}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}^{\sharp}(x),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)\Big)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta)^{2}+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(x)^{(l)},\mathbf{v}\right)+R_{3},

where

R_{3}=R_{3}(\theta_{1},\theta,\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v},\xi)
.

2.6.2
\psi^{\tau}
near
(x_{2},x_{2})

In the neighbourhood of
(x_{2},x_{2})
, we shall consider points of the
form

\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}-t}\big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right),

(52)

where
t\sim 0
; recall that
\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{12})=x_{2}
.

We aim to express the first entry
in (
52
) in NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x_{2}
.
Since
\alpha^{\tau}
is invariant by
the geodesic flow,
\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}
preserves the
vertical and horizontal tangent bundles. In particular, for any

s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{12}}

with some abuse of notation we have

\mathrm{d}_{x_{12}}\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\left(s\,\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x_{12})+\mathbf{v})\right)=s\,\mathcal{R}^{\tau}(x_{2})+B\,\mathbf{v},

(53)

where the symplectic matrix
B
is as in (
25
).

Lemma 67
.

Under the previous assumptions,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}+\big(\theta+R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big).

Before proving Lemma
67
,
let us give a definition.

Definition 68
.

Let
\gamma:(-a,a)\rightarrow X^{\tau}
be a smooth curve
defined for some
a>0
and set
\gamma(0)=x
. Let

k\geq 1
be an integer. The
\gamma
will be
said to be
horizontal to
k
-th order at
x

if
\left\langle\alpha,\dot{\gamma}\right\rangle=O\left(t^{k}\right)
for
t\sim 0
.

Lemma 69
.

In the situation of Definition
68
,
the following conditions are equivalent:

1.

\gamma
is horizontal to second order at
x
, that is,

\left\langle\alpha,\dot{\gamma}\right\rangle=O\left(t^{2}\right)
for
t\sim 0
;

2.

given any system of NHLC’s on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x
,
for an appropriate
\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x}
we have

\gamma(t)=x+\big(R_{3}(t),t\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t)\big).

Proof.

NHLC’s
(\theta,z^{\prime})
on
X^{\tau}
centered at
x
are induced,
under projection and restriction, by NHLC’s

(z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{d-1})
, on
\tilde{M}
adapted to
X^{\tau}

and centered at
x
(see §
2.2
);
here
z_{0}=\theta+\imath\,\eta_{0}
(with
\theta
and
\eta_{0}
real).
By Proposition 33 of
[
P-2024
]
,

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\theta_{0}+\frac{1}{2\,\imath}\,\left[\frac{1}{2\,\tau^{2}}\,\left(\overline{z}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}z_{0}-z_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\overline{z}_{0}\right)+\overline{z}^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}-z^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle+R_{2}(z,\overline{z}).

If
\gamma(t)=\big(\theta(t),z^{\prime}(t)\big)
in local coordinates on
X^{\tau}
, then
\gamma(t)=\big(\theta(t)+\imath\,\eta_{0}(t),z^{\prime}(t)\big)

in the corresponding NHLC’s on
\tilde{M}
.
In particular,
z_{0}(t)=\theta(t)+\imath\,\eta_{0}(t)
.

By Corollary 35 of
[
P-2024
]
,
\eta_{0}(t)=R_{2}(t)
.
Hence

\gamma^{*}\left(\overline{z}_{0}\,\mathrm{d}z_{0}-z_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\overline{z}_{0}\right)=2\,\imath\,\gamma^{*}(\theta\,\mathrm{d}\eta_{0}-\eta_{0}\,\mathrm{d}\theta)=R_{2}(t).

If
z^{\prime}(t)=t\,w+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t)
, then

\displaystyle\gamma^{*}\left(\overline{z}^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}-z^{\prime}\cdot\mathrm{d}\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\left(t\,\overline{w}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t)\right)\cdot\big(w+R_{1}(t)\big)-\left(t\,w+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t)\right)\cdot\big(\overline{w}+R_{1}(t)\big)\right]\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{2}(t).

Hence,

\gamma^{*}(\alpha)=\theta^{\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+R_{2}(t)
.
Thus,
\left\langle\alpha,\dot{\gamma}\right\rangle=R_{2}(t)
if and only if
\theta^{\prime}(t)=R_{2}(t)
if and only if
\theta(t)=R_{3}(t)
.

∎

Proof of Lemma
67
.

By Lemma
60
, we have

x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})=\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\theta/\tau}\Big(x_{12}+\big(R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)\Big),

for certain functions

R_{3}
and
\mathbf{R}_{2}
(with the usual conventions
about vanishing orders, and with
R_{j}
,
\mathbf{R}_{j}
allowed to
vary from line to line).
By (
53
), we can write

\Gamma_{-t_{1}}\Big(x_{12}+\big(R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)\Big)=x_{2}+\big(R_{2}^{(1)}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}^{(1)}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big),

for certain functions vanishing to the indicated orders.

By Lemma
69
,
the smooth curve
\gamma:(-a,a)\rightarrow X^{\tau}
, defined for some

a\geq 1
by

\gamma(t):=x_{12}+\big(R_{3}(t\,\theta,t\,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t\,\theta,t\,\mathbf{v})\big),

is horizontal to second order at
x_{12}
.
Since the geodesic flow
\Gamma^{\tau}
preserves
\alpha^{\tau}
,

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\circ\gamma
is horizontal to second order
at
x_{2}
.
Hence

R_{2}^{(1)}(t\,\theta,t\,\mathbf{v})=O\left(t^{3}\right)
,
and therefore
R_{2}^{(1)}(\theta,\mathbf{v})
really vanishes to
third order at
(0,\mathbf{0})
; we shall accordingly replace it by

R_{3}^{(1)}(\theta,\mathbf{v})
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\theta/\tau}\Big(x_{12}+\big(R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\theta/\tau}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}\Big(x_{12}+\big(R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\theta/\tau}\left(x_{2}+\big(R_{3}^{(1)}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}^{(1)}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}+\Big(\theta+R_{3}^{(2)}(\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}^{(2)}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big),

where in the last equality
we have made use of Lemma
60
.
∎

Using one more time Lemma
60
, we
obtain the following upshot.

Corollary 70
.

With the previous notation,

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t-t_{1}}\Big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}+\Big(\theta+\tau\,t+R_{3}(t,\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t,\theta,\mathbf{v})\Big).

Invoking again Proposition 47 of
[
P-2024
]

(see also §
2.2
above), we conclude that

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}-t}\big(x_{12}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\big),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right)

(54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(x_{2}+\Big(\theta+\tau\,t+R_{3}(t,\theta,\mathbf{v}),B\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(t,\theta,\mathbf{v})\Big),x_{2}+(\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})^{2}+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{v},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)+R_{3}(\theta,\mathbf{v},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}).

3
Proof of Theorem
7

(case of
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
)

3.1
Preamble

In this section, we shall prove Theorem
7

for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
; the formal changes
to the argument needed for

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
will be described in §
5
.
Before giving the proof, we shall lay down
some preliminaries and pieces of notation.

1.

With
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
as in (
10
), we shall denote by

U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}(t):=e^{\imath\,t\,\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}}\qquad(t\in\mathbb{R})

the
1
-parameter group of unitary Toeplitz operators generated by

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
.

2.

The distributional kernel of
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}

in (
13
) is
related to that of
U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}(t)
by

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x,y)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}(t;x,y)\,\mathrm{d}t.

(55)

3.

For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let us denote by

\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}:L^{2}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow L^{2}(X_{\tau})
the operator
having distributional kernel

\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}(x,y):=\Pi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y\right).

4.

Following Zelditch (see e.g.

[
Z-1997
]
and
[
Z-2020
]
),
there exists a zeroth order pseudodifferential operator

P^{\tau}_{t}
on
X^{\tau}
(depending smoothly on
t
), such that

U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}(t)\sim\Pi^{\tau}\circ P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t},

(56)

where
\sim
stands for ‘equal up to smoothing operators’.
More precisely, there exists a classical polyhomogeneous symbol
of the form

\sigma^{\tau}_{t}(x,r)\sim\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\sigma^{\tau}_{t,j}(x)\,r^{-j},

(57)

such that

P^{\tau}_{t}\sim\sigma^{\tau}_{t}\left(x,D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}\right).

The leading order term in (
57
)
(equivalently, the principal symbol of
P^{\tau}_{t}
)
can be described, up to a unitary factor, as
follows.

5.

Let us set
J^{\tau}_{t}:=\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ J\circ\mathrm{d}\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}
. Then
J^{\tau}_{t}
is a new CR structure on
X^{\tau}
,
with corresponding Hardy space

H(X^{\tau})_{t}:={\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}}^{*}\big(H(X^{\tau})\big),

and corresponding Szegő kernel

\tilde{\Pi}^{\tau}_{t}:={\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}}^{*}\circ\Pi^{\tau}\circ{\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}}^{*}.

Thus,
\tilde{\Pi}^{\tau}_{0}=\Pi^{\tau}
; furthermore,
the distributional kernel of
\tilde{\Pi}^{\tau}_{t}
is

\tilde{\Pi}^{\tau}_{t}(x,y):=\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}\times\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}\right)^{*}\left(\Pi^{\tau}(x,y)\right)=\Pi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y)\right).

6.

For every
x\in X^{\tau}
, the vacuum states at
x
associated to
J
and
J_{t}
,
denoted
\sigma_{J}^{(x)}
and
\sigma_{J_{t}}^{(x)}
,
are Gaussian functions on
the horizontal tangent space at
x
.
Their
L^{2}
-pairing

\langle\sigma_{J}^{(x)},\sigma_{J_{t}}^{(x)}\rangle
varies smoothly with
x
and
t
and is nowhere vanishing.
Furthermore, there is a smooth function
\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)
such that

\sigma^{\tau}_{t,0}(x)=e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)}\cdot\langle\sigma_{J}^{(x)},\sigma_{J_{t}}^{(x)}\rangle^{-1}.

(58)

7.

Given the usual description of
\Pi^{\tau}
as an FIO with complex
phase of positive type recalled in section
2.2
, we have

\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}(x,y)\sim\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\imath\,v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y\right)}\,s^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y,v\right)\,\mathrm{d}v.

It follows that the Schwartz kernel of

\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}:=P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}
in (
56
) is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}(x,y)\sim\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{\imath\,v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y\right)}\,r^{\tau}_{t}\left(x,y,v\right)\,\mathrm{d}v

(59)

where

r^{\tau}_{t}\left(x,y,v\right)\sim\sum_{j\geq 0}v^{d-1-j}\,r_{t,j}^{\tau}(x,y),

(60)

and

r_{t,0}^{\tau}(x,y)=\sigma_{t,0}^{\tau}(x)\cdot s^{\tau}_{0}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y\right).

(61)

3.2
The proof

We can now attack the proof of Theorem
7
.
Some of the arguments are an equivariant version of others
in
[
P-2024
]
, but we try to make the exposition
reasonably self-contained.
We shall divide the statement of the Theorem in two parts, and
prove each part separately:

1.

\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)

uniformly for

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1},x_{2}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)})\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}
.

2.

\Pi_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)

uniformly for

\max\left\{\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{1},Z^{\tau}\right),\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{2},Z^{\tau}\right)\right\}\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof of Theorem
7
, Part 1.

By (
18
),
we have for
x_{1},\,x_{2}\in X^{\tau}
:

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim(\nu)\,\int_{G}\,\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,\Pi_{\chi,\lambda}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g).

In view of (
55
), we may reformulate
(
62
) as

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}\left(t;\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)\right].

Therefore, given (
56
), for

\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
we have

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(64)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,\left(\Pi^{\tau}\circ P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}\right)\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)\right];

here,
\sim
means ‘has the same asymptotics as’.

The singular support of
\left(\Pi^{\tau}\circ P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}\right)
is the set of pairs

(x,y)\in X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}
such that

x=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y)
. Hence,
\left(\Pi^{\tau}\circ P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}\right)
is smooth at
\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)

unless
x_{1}=\mu^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})
.

Suppose then that
x_{1}\not\in x_{2}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}
.
Then the function

t\mapsto\chi(t)\cdot\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,\left(\Pi^{\tau}\circ P^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}\right)\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)\right]

is smooth and compactly supported. Hence its Fourier
transform is of rapid decrease.

We conclude the following. Let us define

\mathcal{K}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}:=\left\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}\,:\,x_{1}\in x_{2}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}\right\},

a compact subset of
X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}
.

Lemma 71
.

Let
X^{\prime}\Subset X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}
be any open neighbourhood
of
\mathcal{K}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}
. Then

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)\quad\text{as}\quad\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

uniformly for
(x_{1},x_{2})\not\in X^{\prime}
.

We may thus assume that
x_{1}
belongs to an arbitrarily small
neighbourhood of
x_{2}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}
.
We may rewrite (
64
) as follows:

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(65)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,\Pi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}\left(y,x_{2}\right)\right],

where
\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}
is as in (
59
).

The singular support of
\Pi^{\tau}
is the diagonal in

X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}

[
BdM-S
]
. Hence, only a negligible contribution to
the asymptotics of (
65
) is lost,
if integration in
y
is restricted to a small neighbourhood
of
x_{1}^{G}
. More precisely, let
\varrho_{1}(g,\cdot)
be a cut-off function, smoothly varying with
g
,
identically equal to
1
sufficiently close to

\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1})
,
but vanishing identically outside a small open neighbourhood
of the point. Then only a rapidly decreasing contribution to
the asymptotics is lost, if the integrand in
(
65
) is multiplied by
\varrho_{1}(g,y)
.
Similarly, the singular support of

\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}
in (
59
)
is the set of pairs
(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
with

x^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x^{\prime\prime})
. Again, we conclude that the
asymptotics of (
65
) will be unaltered,
if the integrand is further multiplied by
\varrho_{2}(t,y)
,
where
\varrho_{2}(t,\cdot)
varies smoothly with
t
,
is supported on a small neighbourhood of

\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})
, and identically equal to
1

sufficiently close to it.

Given this, the pairs
\big(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\big)

and
\big(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\big)
belong to small
neighbourhoods of the diagonal. Therefore, on the domain of
integration we may replace
\Pi^{\tau}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{t}

by the representations as FIO’s with complex phase
[
BdM-S
]
,
perhaps at the cost of losing a negligible contribution to
the asymptotics.

Thus, as
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
,

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(66)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,e^{\imath\,u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+\imath\,v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\varrho_{1}(g,y)\,\varrho_{2}(t,y)\,s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\left(y,x_{2},v\right)\right],

where
r^{\tau}_{t}
is as in (
60
).
Let us now operate the rescaling

u\mapsto\lambda\,u,\,v\mapsto\lambda\,v
:

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(67)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,\chi(t)\,e^{\imath\,\lambda\,\left[u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\right)-t\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\varrho_{1}(g,y)\,\varrho_{2}(t,y)\,s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y,\lambda\,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\left(y,x_{2},\lambda\,v\right)\right].

Let us set

\Psi(x_{1},x_{2};g,t,y,u,v):=u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\right)-t.

(68)

The next step will be to argue that integration in

u
and
v
may be restricted to certain compact
neighbourhoods of
1/\tau
in
\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

Proposition 72
.

There exist cut-off functions
f_{1},\,f_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, identically equal to
1
near
1/\tau
,
such that the asymptotics of (
67
) are
unaltered, if the integral is multiplied by

f_{1}(v)\cdot f_{2}(u)
.

For notational simplicity,
the cut-off
f_{1}(v)\cdot f_{2}(u)
will be implicitly absorbed
in the amplitude.

Proof.

As remarked, by the previous reductions,
on the domain of integration

\big(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\big)

and
\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y,)x_{2}\right)
now belong to
a small
neighbourhood of the diagonal of,
say, tubular radius
\delta>0
.
Hence, in local coordinates we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\big(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),\,y\big)}\psi^{\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha^{\tau}_{\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1})},-\alpha^{\tau}_{\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1})}\right)+O(\delta),

(69)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),\,x_{2}\right)}\psi^{\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha^{\tau}_{x_{2}},-\alpha^{\tau}_{x_{2}}\right)+O(\delta).

Localizing the computation near some

\left(\tilde{g},\tilde{t}\right)
, we shall set

\tilde{x}:=\mu^{\tau}_{\tilde{g}^{-1}}(x_{1})

(so that there is a naturally induced system of NHLC’s
centered at
\tilde{x}
)
and

g:=\tilde{g}\,e^{\xi},\quad y=\tilde{x}+(\theta,\mathbf{v}),\quad t=\tilde{t}+a.

In local coordinates we get:

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y)=\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a}\circ\Gamma_{-\tilde{t}}(y)=\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a}(x_{2})+O(\delta).

Therefore, we conclude from
Lemma
60
that

\partial_{t}\Psi=v\,\tau-1+O(\delta).

Since the variable
t
is compactly supported, it is legitimate to
integrate by parts in
t
, and we conclude that the contribution
to the asymptotics of (
67
) of the locus
where
0<v\ll 1/\tau
or
v\gg 1/\tau
is rapidly decreasing.

More precisely, we conclude the following.

Lemma 73
.

The asymptotics of (
67
) are unchanged, if
the integrand is multiplied by
f_{1}(v)
, where

f_{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+})
is identically
equal to
1
on a suitable neighbourhood of
1/\tau
.

In the following, to simplify notation the cut-off
f_{1}(v)

will be absorbed in the amplitude of (
67
).

Let us adopts NHLC’s centered at

\tilde{x}:=\mu^{\tau}_{\tilde{g}^{-1}}(x_{1})
, and write

y=\tilde{x}+(\theta,\mathbf{v}),

where
\lVert(\theta,\mathbf{v})\rVert=O(\delta)
.
Then

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)=\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{e^{-\xi}}\left(\tilde{x}\right),\tilde{x}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\right)

(70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\tilde{x}+\Big(\langle\Phi(\tilde{x}),\xi\rangle+R_{3}(\xi),-\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(\tilde{x})+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\xi)\Big),\tilde{x}+(\theta,\mathbf{v})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,\big(\langle\Phi(\tilde{x}),\xi\rangle-\theta\big)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,\big(\langle\Phi(\tilde{x}),\xi\rangle-\theta\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\xi^{\sharp}_{X^{\tau}}(\tilde{x})\right\|^{2}+R_{3}(\xi,\theta).

Let us fix a system of NHLC’s at

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x})
.
Furthermore, arguing as in Lemma
67
, for a suitable symplectic matrix
M_{\tilde{t}}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a-\tilde{t}}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}\Big(\tilde{x}+(\theta,\mathbf{v}\big)\Big)

(71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x})+\Big(\theta+R_{3}(\theta),M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v})\Big)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x})+\Big(\theta+\tau\,a+R_{3}(\theta,a),M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v},a)\Big)

By assumption

\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x})=x_{2}+\big(R_{1}(\delta),\mathbf{R}_{1}(\delta)\big)
.
Hence by Corollary
62

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\tau}_{-a-\tilde{t}}(y)=\Gamma^{\tau}_{-\tilde{t}}(\tilde{x})+\Big(\theta+\tau\,a+R_{3}(\theta,a),M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v},a)\Big)

(72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(x_{2}+\big(R_{1}(\delta),\mathbf{R}_{1}(\delta)\big)\Big)+\Big(\theta+\tau\,a+R_{3}(\theta,a),M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v},a)\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}+\Big(\theta+\tau\,a+R_{3}(\theta,a)+R_{1}(\delta),M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{R}_{1}(\delta)+\mathbf{R}_{2}(\theta,\mathbf{v},a)\Big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\imath\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma_{-a-\tilde{t}}(y),x_{2}\right)

(73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\imath\,(\theta+\tau\,a)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta+\tau\,a)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|M_{\tilde{t}}\,\mathbf{v}\|^{2}+R_{2}(\delta,a).

(74)

Given that
|a|,\,|\theta|\leq C\,(\epsilon+\delta)
, by (
68
)
we conclude that

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}\Psi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-u+v+O(\epsilon+\delta).

(75)

The variable
\theta
being also compactly supported,
and
v
ranges in a certain neighbourhood of
1/\tau
;
therefore, upon choosing
\epsilon
and
\delta

suitably small, we conclude that the contribution to the
asymptotics of the locus where
0<u\ll 1/\tau
or

u\gg 1/\tau
is also negligible. In other words, the
asymptotics of (
67
) are unaltered,
if the integrand is multiplied by a cut-off function of the
form
f_{2}(u)
, where
f_{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+})

is identically equal to
1
on an appropriate neighbourhood of

1/\tau
.

The proof of Proposition
72
is complete.

∎

By Corollary 1.3 in
[
BdM-S
]
, there exists a constant

C^{\tau}>0
such that for any
x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\tau}

\left|\psi^{\tau}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\right|\geq\Im\left(\psi^{\tau}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\right)\geq C^{\tau}\,\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})^{2}.

Let
\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}=\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}(x_{1};g,y)

be a smooth function
identically equal to
1
where

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)<a^{\tau}\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}

and vanishes where
\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)>A^{\tau}\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
, for certain constants

A^{\tau}>a^{\tau}>0
.
Similarly, let

\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}=\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2};t,y)

be a smooth function
identically equal to
1
where

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y,)x_{2}\right)<b^{\tau}\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}

and vanishes where
\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y,)x_{2}\right)>B^{\tau}\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
, for certain constants

B^{\tau}>b^{\tau}>0
.

Lemma 74
.

The asymptotics of (
67
) are unaltered, if the
integrand is further multiplied by

\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}(x_{1};g,y)\,\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2};t,y)
.

Proof.

On the support of

1-\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}
, we can write

e^{\imath\,\lambda\,u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)}=-\frac{\imath}{\lambda}\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)^{-1}\,\partial_{u}\left(e^{\imath\,\lambda\,u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)}\right).

Furthermore, for some constant
D^{\tau}>0

\left|\frac{\imath}{\lambda}\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)^{-1}\right|\leq D^{\tau}\lambda^{-2\,\epsilon^{\prime}}.

Hence, by iteratively integrating by parts in the compactly
supported variable
u
we conclude that the contribution to
the asymptotics of the locus where

\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}\neq 1
is
O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)
.

The argument for
\rho^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}
is similar.
∎

Let us choose
r>1
such that

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq r\,\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x^{\prime}),\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x^{\prime\prime})\right)\quad\forall\,x^{\prime},\,x^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\tau},\quad-t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi).

On the support of the product
\chi(t)\cdot\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}(x_{1};g,y)\,\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2};t,y)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{1},\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x_{2})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})\right)

(76)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r\,\left[\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}\cdot r\,\left(A^{\tau}+B^{\tau}\right).

Given any
C>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}>0
, on the other hand,
for
0<\epsilon^{\prime\prime}<\epsilon^{\prime}
and
\lambda\gg 0
one has

\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}-1/2}\cdot r\,\left(A^{\tau}+B^{\tau}\right)<C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}.

This completes the proof of Part 1.
of Theorem
7
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
7
, Part 2.

By the arguments in the proof of Part 1., integration
in (
67
)
may be restricted to the locus where

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)\leq A\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
.
Hence we may refine (
69
) to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\big(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\big)}\psi^{\tau}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\alpha^{\tau}_{\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1})},-\alpha^{\tau}_{\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1})}\right)+O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(77)

Working locally near a given
g_{0}\in G
, we can write

g=g_{0}\,e^{\xi}
, where
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
.
In view of Corollary
48
and (
68
),
we conclude that on the domain of integration

\partial_{\xi}\Psi=u\,\langle\Phi(x_{1}),\xi\rangle+O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

(recall Notation
49
).
In other words,

\partial_{g}\Psi=u\,\Phi(x_{1})+O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Since
u
is now bounded away from zero, we conclude by
iteratively integrating by parts in
g
that the contribution to
the asymptotics of
the locus where

\|\Phi(x_{1})\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}

is negligible. Given that by assumption

0
is a regular value of
\Phi
,
we conclude that

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}\right),

uniformly for
\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1},Z^{\tau})\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
. The similar statement for

x_{2}
in the following way: set
\chi_{-}(t):=\chi(-t)
, so that
\overline{\hat{\chi}}=\widehat{\chi_{-}}
, then by definition we have:

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\overline{\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})}\,\sum_{k}\rho^{(\nu)}_{j,k}(x_{2})\cdot\overline{\rho^{(\nu)}_{j,k}(x_{1})}

(78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\widehat{\chi_{-}}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\sum_{k}\rho^{(\nu)}_{j,k}(x_{2})\cdot\overline{\rho^{(\nu)}_{j,k}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi_{-},\nu,\lambda}(x_{2},x_{1}).

Applying a similar argument to
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi_{-},\nu,\lambda}(x_{2},x_{1})
yields the same conclusion for
x_{2}
.
∎

4
Scaling asymptotics for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

In this section, we shall prove Theorems

15
(for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
),

25
, and
28
; the adaptations required
for
P_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
will be dealt with in
§
5
.
Before discussing the proofs, let us interject some recalls
and remarks.

If
x_{1}\in Z^{\tau}
and
x_{1}\in x_{2}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}
,
then
x_{2}\in Z^{\tau}
and
the action of
G\times\mathbb{R}
is locally free at
both
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
; furthermore,

x_{1}^{G}\cap x_{2}^{\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}=\{x_{12}\}
for a unique point
x_{12}
(Corollary
58
),
and
\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
is as in (
39
).
Let us fix NHLC’s at
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.
Given
h_{1}
as in (
39
), we obtain from
the NHLC’s at
x_{1}
a system of NHLC’s at
x_{12}=\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}(x_{1})
, simply by composing with

\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}}
.

We shall first set the common stage for the
proofs, and then specialize the argument for each Theorem separately.

Remark 75
.

Having chosen (normal) Heisenberg local coordinates
at
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, in the
following computations we may replace
\omega_{x_{j}}
(restricted
to
\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{j}}
) by the standard symplectic form

\omega_{0}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
(see
(63) of
[
P-2024
]
).

4.1
General arguments for the scaling asymptotics
of
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}

With the aim to study the asymptotics of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})
,
with
x_{j,\lambda}
as in (
21
),
we start from
(
67
), with
x_{j,\lambda}
in place of

x_{j}
. The following arguments will eventually depend on an
application of the Stationary Phase Lemma, and all the previously
introduced cut-offs are identically
equal to
1
near the stationary point. With abuse of notation,
we shall
occasionally implicitly
absorb the cut-offs in the amplitude,
unless it is relevant for the argument to do otherwise.
Thus we may write

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(79)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[e^{\imath\,\lambda\,\Psi(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda};g,t,y,u,v)}\,\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,\chi(t)\,s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1,\lambda}),y,\lambda\,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\left(y,x_{2,\lambda},\lambda\,v\right)\right].

where

\Psi(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda};g,t,y,u,v)=u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1,\lambda}),y\right)+v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2,\lambda}\right)-t.

(80)

By the previous reductions, integration has been reduced to a
locus where

\max\left\{\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1,\lambda}),y\right),\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2,\lambda}\right)\right\}=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right);

hence, we also have

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})\right)=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Since the action of
G\times\mathbb{R}
on
Z^{\tau}

is locally free, this entails that (once the previous cut-offs have been taken into account)
(g,t)
ranges in a neighbourhood
of radius
O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}\right)
of the set

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
in (
38
)
and (
39
). We can reformulate this
slightly more explicitly as follows.

Let

\gamma^{\mathfrak{g}}:\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

denote a bump function
supported in a small neighbourhood of
0\in\mathfrak{g}
,
and identically equal to
1
sufficiently
close to
0
.
Similarly, let

\gamma^{\mathbb{R}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
denote a compactly
supported bump function compactly supported on a small
neighbourhood of
0\in\mathbb{R}
, and identically equal
to
1
sufficiently close to
0
.
We conclude that the asymptotics of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

are unaltered, if the integrand in (
79
)
is further multiplied by the expression

\gamma^{\mathbb{R}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}+1/2}\,(t-t_{1})\right)\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{r}\gamma^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}+1/2}\,\log_{G}\left(g\,h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\right)\right),

(81)

where
\log_{G}=\exp_{G}^{-1}
is defined on some neighbourhood
of
e_{G}
.
The cut-off (
81
) plays the same role as
the product
\chi(t)\cdot\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime}(x_{1};g,y)\,\rho_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}(x_{2};t,y)
preceding
(
76
), and may replace it in
the integrand.

If we multiply the integrand in (
79
)
by the cut-off in (
81
), we obtain

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r}\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

(82)

where
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

is given by right hand side of
(
79
), with the integrand
multiplied by the
l
-th summand in (
81
).

We are thus reduced to computing the asymptotics of each
summand in (
82
). To this end,
let us make the change of variables

t\mapsto t_{1}+t,\,g=e^{\xi}\,\kappa_{l}\,h_{1},

where
(t,\xi)
now ranges in a neighbourhood
of the origin in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathfrak{g}
.
The
l
-th summand in (
81
) now takes the
form

\gamma^{\mathbb{R}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}+1/2}\,t\right)\cdot\gamma^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}+1/2}\,\xi\right).

(83)

On the current domain of integration we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{12}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{\kappa_{l}^{-1}}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{e^{-\xi}}(x_{1}),\mu^{\tau}_{h_{1}^{-1}}(x_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right),

and
\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
, whence also

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,x_{12}\right)=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\right)
.
We can then express
y
in NHLC’s at
x_{12}
as

y=x_{12}+\left(\theta,\mathbf{u}\right),

where
\|(\theta,\mathbf{u})\|\leq C^{\prime}\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}
for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
.
By (67) and (117) of

[
P-2024
]
,

\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)=\mathcal{V}(\theta,\mathbf{u})\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u},\quad\mathcal{V}(0,\mathbf{0})=\frac{2^{d-1}}{\tau}.

(84)

Let us operate the rescaling

(\theta,\,\mathbf{u},\,\xi,\,t)\mapsto\left(\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\,\frac{\xi}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\,\frac{t}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right);

(85)

and accordingly rewrite the parametrization of
y
as

y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}):=x_{12}+\left(\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

(86)

In the rescaled variables, the cut-off
(
83
) takes the form

\gamma^{\mathbb{R}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,t\right)\cdot\gamma^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,\xi\right),

(87)

so that integration in
(\xi,t)
is now over a ball centered
at the origin and radius
O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)

in
\mathfrak{g}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Similarly, since prior to rescaling

(\theta,\mathbf{v})
range in a shrinking ball centered at
the origin of radius
O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}\right)
,
the rescaled variables will range in an expanding ball
of radius
O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)
in

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
.

Let
\mathrm{d}\xi
denote the Lebesgue measure on

\mathfrak{g}
associated to a bi-invariant metric on

G
whose Riemannian density is the Haar measure.
We obtain

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

(88)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda^{2-d-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[e^{\imath\,\lambda\,\Psi_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{v},u,v)_{l}}\,\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u},u,v)_{l}\right],

where, recalling (
68
),

\displaystyle\Psi_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u},u,v)_{l}

(89)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Psi\left(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda};e^{\xi/\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\kappa_{l}\,h_{1},t_{1}+\frac{t}{\sqrt{\lambda}},y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}),u,v\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u},u,v)_{l}

(90)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Xi_{\nu}\left(h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\,e^{-\xi/\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\,\chi\left(t_{1}+\frac{t}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\cdot\mathcal{V}\left(\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{(e^{\xi/\sqrt{\lambda}}\kappa_{l}h_{1})^{-1}}(x_{1,\lambda}),y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}),\lambda\,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\big(y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}),x_{2,\lambda},\lambda\,v\big)

\displaystyle\cdot\tilde{\gamma}_{\lambda}(t,\theta,\xi,\mathbf{u})\cdot f_{1}(v)\cdot f_{2}(u);

here
\mathcal{V}
is as in (
84
),
and we have collected in
\tilde{\gamma}

the cut-offs in the rescaled variables (which may be assumed to be
all of the form (
87
)); finally,

f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are as in Proposition

72
.

The next step will be to expand (
89
) in
descending powers of
\lambda
.
Recalling (
80
), we shall first expand the
individual summands. In view of (
51
),

\displaystyle\imath\,u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{(e^{\xi/\sqrt{\lambda}}\kappa_{l}h_{1})^{-1}}(x_{1,\lambda}),y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u})\right)

(91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\imath}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,u\,(\theta_{1}-\theta)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda}\,u\,\left[\imath\,\omega_{x_{1}}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta)^{2}+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+u\,R_{3}\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{v}_{1}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{t}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\xi}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right);

in the following, we shall replace

\omega_{x_{j}}
by
\omega_{0}
(Remark
75
),
and abridge
k
-th order
remainder terms such as the
one on the latter line of (
91
) by
the short-hand
R_{k}(\bullet/\sqrt{\lambda})
.

Regarding the second summand in (
80
), in view of (
54
) we have

\displaystyle\imath\,v\,\psi\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}-t/\sqrt{\lambda}}\left(x_{12}+\left(\frac{\theta}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{u}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\right),x_{2}+\left(\frac{\theta_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\frac{\mathbf{v}_{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\right)

(92)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\imath}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\,v\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{v}{\lambda}\,\left[-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})^{2}+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]+R_{3}\left(\frac{\bullet}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right).

The upshot is an expansion for (
89
) of the form

\displaystyle\imath\,\lambda\,\Psi_{\lambda}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u},u,v)_{l}

(93)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}+\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u)+\mathcal{S}(x,\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}

\displaystyle+\lambda\,R_{3}\left(\frac{\bullet}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right),

where:

\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u):=u\,(\theta_{1}-\theta)+v\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})-t,

(94)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}

(95)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle u\,\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta)^{2}+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+v\,\left[-\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2})^{2}+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right].

Let us set

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}

(96)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}\,e^{\lambda\,R_{3}\left(\frac{\bullet}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)}\cdot\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u},u,v)_{l}.

Then (
88
)
may be rewritten as

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

(97)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\lambda^{2-d-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\big[I_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}\big],

where

\displaystyle I_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}

(98)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u)}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right].

We can now pair Taylor expansion
in the rescaled variables in (
90
) and
in the factor
e^{\lambda\,R_{3}\left(\bullet/\sqrt{\lambda}\right)}

with the asymptotic expansions of the classical symbols
s^{\tau}
and
r^{\tau}_{t}
.
The same arguments leading to the asymptotic expansion (142)
of
[
P-2024
]
in the action-free case yields a similar expansion
in the present setting.
Before stating it, let us make the following remarks regarding
the leading order terms in
s^{\tau}
and
r^{\tau}_{t}
in NHLC’s.

First, by Theorem
51
, in NHLC’s at
x_{12}

we have

\displaystyle s^{\tau}_{0}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{(\kappa_{l}h_{1})^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{12}\right)=s^{\tau}_{0}\left(x_{12},x_{12}\right)=\frac{\tau}{(2\,\pi)^{d}}.

(99)

Second, recalling (
61
),

\displaystyle r_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{12},x_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{12})\cdot s^{\tau}_{0}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{12}),x_{2}\right)

(100)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot s^{\tau}_{0}\left(x_{2},x_{2}\right)=\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\frac{\tau}{(2\,\pi)^{d}},

where the front factor is as in (
58
).
We then have the following.

Lemma 76
.

As
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, there is an asymptotic
expansion

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)}\cdot\frac{2^{d-1}}{\tau}\cdot\lambda^{2\,(d-1)}\cdot(u\,v)^{d-1}\cdot\frac{\tau^{2}}{(2\,\pi)^{2d}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\beta\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,(t,\xi,\theta,\mathbf{u})\right)\cdot f_{1}(u)\cdot f_{2}(v)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[1+\sum_{k\geq 1}\lambda^{-k/2}\,B_{k}(u,v,\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right],

where
B_{k}(u,v,\cdot)
is a polynomial in the rescaled variables,
of degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k
.

The latter is indeed an asymptotics expansion for
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
.

Integration in
u
and
v
is compactly supported. Furthermore,
in view of (
94
)

\partial_{u}\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u)=\theta_{1}-\theta,\quad\partial_{v}\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u)=\theta+\tau\,t-\theta_{2}.

Hence the partial differential

\partial_{u,v}\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{u,v}\Psi^{\tau}_{\theta_{1},\theta_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u)\right\|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C_{\tau}\,\left\|\left(\theta-\theta_{1},t-\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\tau}\right)\right\|.

For any given

\delta>0
,
by iteratively integrating by parts in
(u,v)
on
the locus where
\left\|\big(\theta-\theta_{1},(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})/\tau\big)\right\|\geq\delta
, one introduces at
each step a factor
O\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\right)
.
On the other hand, the radius
of domain of
integration in (
98
)
grows like
\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
; furthermore, once divided by

\lambda^{2\,(d-1)}
the amplitude

\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}

remains bounded
on the latter domain by Lemma
76
and
(
95
).
One then has the following.

Lemma 77
.

Only a rapidly decreasing contribution to the asymptotics of
(
98
) - and
(
97
) - is lost, if integration
in
(\theta,t)
is restricted to a fixed and arbitrarily small
neighbourhood of
\big(\theta_{1},(\theta_{2}-\theta_{1})/\tau\big)
.

Leaving a corresponding cut-off function in
(\theta,t)
implicit,
we may now study the asymptotics of
I_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}

in (
98
)
using the stationary phase Lemma.
The phase (
89
) has already been considered in [Grauert-9-2023] (where it is denoted
\Upsilon^{\tau}
).
By Lemma 64 of the same paper,
(
94
) has a unique stationary point

P_{s}=(t_{s},v_{s},\theta_{s},u_{s})
, given by

P_{s}=\left(\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\tau},\frac{1}{\tau},\theta_{1},\frac{1}{\tau}\right);

(101)

furthermore, the Hessian determinant and signature at the
critical point are, respectively,
\tau^{2}
and
0
.
Arguing as in §4 of
[Grauert] (in particular, as in the derivation
of (147) in
loc. cit.
)
we then obtain an asymptotic expansion for

I_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}
in (
98
)
of the following form:

\displaystyle I_{\lambda}(x_{j},\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}

(102)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2\,\pi}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\tau}\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]}

\displaystyle\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)}\cdot\frac{2^{d-1}}{\tau}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{2\,(d-1)}\cdot\frac{\tau^{2}}{(2\,\pi)^{2d}}

\displaystyle\cdot\beta^{\prime}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,(\xi,\mathbf{u})\right)\cdot\left[1+\sum_{k\geq 1}\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k}^{\tau}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi^{2}\,\tau^{2})^{d-1}}\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)}\cdot e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}

\displaystyle\cdot\beta^{\prime}\left(\lambda^{-\epsilon^{\prime}}\,(\xi,\mathbf{u})\right)\cdot\left[1+\sum_{k\geq 1}\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right]

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}

(103)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right],

\beta^{\prime}
is a cut-off identically equal to
1
near the origin, and

F_{k}(\cdot)_{l}
is a polynomial in the indicated variables,
of degree
\leq 3\,k
and parity
k

(and implicitly depending on
x_{1},\,x_{2}
).

More precisely, let us set

L:=\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\,\partial\,u}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial t\,\partial\,v}\right)-\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\theta\,\partial\,u}.

Then,
when applying
the Stationary Phase Lemma to (
98
),
the
k
-th summand in the asymptotic expansion of the amplitude in
Lemma
76
will yield an asymptotic expansion
whose
j
-th term (where
j\geq 0
) is a multiple of

\displaystyle\frac{4\,\pi^{2}}{\tau}\,\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)}\cdot\frac{2^{d-1}}{\tau}\cdot\lambda^{2d-3-\frac{k+j}{2}}\cdot\frac{\tau^{2}}{(2\,\pi)^{2d}}

\displaystyle\left.L^{j}\left(e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}\cdot(u\,v)^{d-1}\cdot B_{k}(u,v,\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right)\right|_{P_{s}}.

Using that
\mathcal{S}
is homogeneous of degree two in the rescaled variables, one can check
inductively that the resulting expansion has the stated form.

Again in view of the cut-offs and the exponential, the
asymptotic expansion (
102
)
may be integrated term by term. Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\big[I_{\lambda}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}\big]

(104)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi^{2}\,\tau^{2})^{d-1}}\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\lambda^{-k/2}\mathcal{I}_{k}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{k}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(105)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\left[e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}\cdot F_{k}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}\right];

we have set
F_{0}=1
.

Let us first consider the leading order terms in (
104
)
and in (
97
),
bearing in mind the direct sum decompositions of

T_{x}X^{\tau}
induced by
\alpha^{\tau}
at any
x\in X^{\tau}
, and
by
\mu^{\tau}
at any
x\in Z^{\tau}
(see (
8
)
and Definition
18
).

Since in NHLC’s we are
unitarily identifying

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{1}}\cong\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{12}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
(recall Notation
20
above), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{v}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2-2d_{G}}_{h}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}.

Furthermore, let us make the following remarks on
the geodesic flow
\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}:X^{\tau}\rightarrow X^{\tau}
:

1.

\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}
preserves
\alpha
and commutes with
\mu^{\tau}
,
hence it preserves the vector bundle decompositions (
8
)
on
X^{\tau}
and in Definition
18
on
Z^{\tau}
;

2.

for any
\xi\in\mathfrak{g}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
the induced
vector field
\xi_{X^{\tau}}

is self-correlated under
\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}
.

Let us consider the case
k=0
in
(
105
).
We have (Remark
75
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(106)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\left[e^{\mathcal{S}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2};\mathbf{u},\xi)_{l}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\left[e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}\big)+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{(l)}_{1}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]}\right].

Let us set

\mathbf{v}_{j}(\tau):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tau}}\,\mathbf{v}_{j},

(107)

and perform the change of coordinates

\xi\rightarrow\sqrt{\tau}\,\xi,\quad\mathbf{u}\rightarrow\sqrt{\tau}\,\mathbf{u}.

We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\left[e^{\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\big)+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)}\right].

We have

\displaystyle\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1}),\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)\big)+\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)

(109)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)-\imath\,\omega_{x}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle+\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}.

We first compute the
\xi
-integral. We can transfer the
integral over
\mathfrak{g}
to
T_{x_{1}}^{v}X^{\tau}\cong\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}
, the tangent space to

x_{1}^{G}
. If
r=|G_{x_{1}}|
, the action map
G\rightarrow G\cdot x_{1}

is
r_{x_{1}}:1
(
r_{x_{1}}=|G_{x_{1}}|
).
We then have the replacement

\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\quad\text{by}\quad\frac{1}{r_{x_{1}}\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\int_{T_{x_{1}}^{v}X^{\tau}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}^{v}=\frac{1}{r_{x_{1}}\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{v}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{a}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi

(110)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{x_{1}}\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}e^{\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{a}^{(l)},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{a}^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r_{x_{1}}\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot e^{-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}}.

We can insert (
110
) in (
108
)
and obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(111)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,e^{A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u},

where

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)

(112)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}^{h}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big).

4.2
The leading order term in the action free case

In this section, we shall determine the leading order term in
in Theorem
15
. To avoid repetitions, we shall
later give a general argument for the lower order terms covering the
general equivariant case.

Determination of the leading order term in Theorem
15
.

In the action-free case, we have
\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{h}
, and
the suffix
l
may be omitted. Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})=\tau^{d-1}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,e^{F(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u},

(113)

where

\displaystyle F\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u}\big)=\psi_{2}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{u}\big)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big).

As is Definition
8
, following
[
F
]
,
let us write

B^{-1}_{c}=\begin{pmatrix}P&Q\\
\overline{Q}&\overline{P}\end{pmatrix},

(114)

where
P
is invertible and
\|P\mathbf{v}\|\geq\|\mathbf{v}\|

for every
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
.
Furthermore, let us choose a metaplectic lift

\tilde{B}^{-1}
of
B^{-1}
, and denote by
K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}
the
integral kernel of the metaplectic representation of

\tilde{B}^{-1}
. With the present normalizations
(here

\omega_{0}=(\imath/2)\,\sum_{j}\mathrm{d}z_{j}\wedge\mathrm{d}\overline{z}_{j}
),
it follows from
the discussion in §4 of
[
ZZ-2018
]
(and Ch. 4 of
[
F
]
)
that, with the notation of Definition
9
,
K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}:\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

is
given by

\displaystyle K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}(Z,W):=\pi^{-(d-1)}\cdot\det(P)^{-1/2}\cdot\exp\left[\Psi_{B_{c}^{-1}}(Z,W)\right],

(115)

the square root being well-defined at the metaplectic level.
We may alternatively view
K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}
as defined on

K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}:\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

On the other hand, let
\Pi_{1}
be the integral kernel of the
level-
1
Szegő kernel on
(\mathbb{C}^{d-1},\omega_{0})
with the
standard polarization.
Explicitly, this is given by

\Pi_{1}(Z,W):=\pi^{-(d-1)}\,e^{\psi_{2}(Z,W)}.

where
\psi_{2}
is as in Definition
11

and Notation
12

(there is some abuse of language here, since the Szegő kernel
is really defined on
X\times X
, where

X\cong\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\times S^{1}
is an appropriate
unit circle bundle on
\mathbb{C}^{d-1}
, and
\Pi_{1}

above is in fact its pull-back under the map

Z\mapsto(Z,1)
). Again, we can equivalently view

\Pi_{1}
as defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
.

The relation between
K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}

and
\Pi_{1}
is described in Proposition 4.4 of
[
ZZ-2018
]

(building on the theory of
[
D
]
).
Namely, if
Z,\,W\in\mathbb{C}^{d-1}

correspond to
\mathbf{v},\,\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}
, then

\displaystyle K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}(Z,W)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{1,\tilde{B}^{-1}}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})

(116)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det(P^{*})^{1/2}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\Pi_{1}(\mathbf{v},B^{-1}\mathbf{u})\,\Pi_{1}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}.

Given (
115
) and (
116
), we may rewrite
(
113
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau^{d-1}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2(d-1)}}\,e^{\psi_{2}(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{u})+\psi_{2}(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau))}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

(117)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{2(d-1)}\,\tau^{d-1}\,\int_{\mathbb{C}^{d-1}}\,\Pi_{1}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{u}\big)\,\Pi_{1}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{2(d-1)}\,\tau^{d-1}\,\int_{\mathbb{C}^{d-1}}\,\Pi_{1}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),B^{-1}\mathbf{u}\big)\,\Pi_{1}\big(\,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{2(d-1)}\,\tau^{d-1}\,\det(P^{*})^{-1/2}\,K_{1,\tilde{B}_{c}^{-1}}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\pi\,\tau)^{d-1}\,|\det(P)|^{-1}\,\cdot\exp\left[\Psi_{B^{-1}}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)\right].

Using (
117
), we obtain that to leading
order (
104
) is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\big[I_{\lambda}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}\big]

(118)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi\,\tau)^{d-1}}\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})

\displaystyle\cdot|\det(P)|^{-1}\,\cdot\exp\left[\frac{1}{\tau}\,\Psi_{B^{-1}}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]

Lemma 78
.

Let the unitary factor
e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)}
be
as in (
58
). Then

\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\left|\det(P)\right|^{-1}=e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}.

Proof of Lemma
78
.

Let
B
be as in (
25
). In view of (
114
)
and the computations in Ch. 4 of
[
F
]
(especially Proposition 4.17) we have

B_{c}=\begin{pmatrix}P^{*}&-Q^{\dagger}\\
-Q^{*}&P^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix}.

Let us write
B
in square block form:

B=\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}&B^{\prime}\\
C^{\prime}&D^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}.

It follows from the computation in the proof of Lemma
3.3 of
[
Z-2020
]
(based on
[
D
]
) that,
with the notation in (
58
),

\displaystyle\langle\sigma_{J}^{(x)},\sigma_{J_{t_{1}}}^{(x_{1})}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{d-1}}{\left|\det\big(A^{\prime}+D^{\prime}+\imath\,(B^{\prime}-C^{\prime})\big)\right|}

(119)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{d-1}}{\left|\det\big(2\,P\big)\right|}=\frac{1}{|\det(P)|},

where on the last line we have made use of (54) of
[
Z-2020
]
.
The claim follows.
∎

Thus, in the action-free case,
going back to (
97
) we obtain
that to leading order

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(121)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\lambda^{2-d}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi\,\tau)^{d-1}}\cdot\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(t_{1})

\displaystyle\cdot|\det(P)|^{-1}\,\cdot\exp\left[\frac{1}{\tau}\,\Psi_{B^{-1}}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\frac{\chi(t_{1})}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1}\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\left\{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})+\Psi_{B^{-1}}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)\right]\right\},

as claimed (with
\theta^{\tau}(x_{1},x_{2})=\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})
).

∎

4.3
Proof of Theorem
25

Let us return to the general equivariant setting.

Proof of statement 1 of Theorem
25
.

We are assuming
\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}
.
As in Definition
18
, let us decompose

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}^{h}+\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{v}
.
Then (
112
) reduces to

\displaystyle A\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u}\big)_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}

(122)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\big);

in particular, in this case

A(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{u})_{l}

is independent of
l
.
Hence (
111
) may be rewritten

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(123)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot A_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right),

where
A_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right)
is independent of

\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau)

(and
l
).

Let
A_{\chi}:\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be as in Definition
22
.

Lemma 79
.

Let
\chi_{-}(t):=\chi(-t)
.
With the previous hypothesis and notation,

\displaystyle\overline{e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot A_{\chi_{-}}(x_{2},x_{1}),

\displaystyle e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot A_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2}).

Proof.

Let
\chi_{-}(t)=\chi(-t)
then

\overline{\hat{\chi}}=\widehat{\chi_{-}}
and

\chi_{-}\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}\big([-t_{0}-\epsilon,-t_{0}+\epsilon]\big)
. Referring to (
38
),

\Sigma_{\chi_{-}}(x_{2},x_{1})=\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})^{-1},

(124)

where inversion is meant in the group
G\times\mathbb{R}
.

The computation in (
78
) yields:

\overline{\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})}=\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi_{-},\nu,\lambda}(x_{2,\lambda},x_{1,\lambda}).

(125)

By assumption,
G_{x_{2}}=h_{1}^{-1}\,G_{x_{1}}\,h_{1}
.
Suppose
G_{x_{1}}=\left\{\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{r}\right\}
;
then

G_{x_{2}}=\left\{h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{1}\,h_{1},\ldots,h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{r}\,h_{1}\right\}=\left\{\tilde{\kappa}_{1},\ldots,\tilde{\kappa}_{r}\right\},

where
\tilde{\kappa}_{j}:=h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{j}^{-1}\,h_{1}
.
For every
l=1,\ldots,r
,

\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{l}\,h_{1}^{-1}\right)}=\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\,h_{1}\,h_{1}^{-1}\right)}=\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\right)}=\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right).

Let us apply (
97
),
(
104
), and
(
111
) with
x_{1,\lambda}
and
x_{2,\lambda}

swapped and
\chi
replaced by
\chi_{-}
.
Then
t_{1}
is replaced with
-t_{1}
, and

B
with
B^{-1}
; hence

P
gets replaced with
P^{*}
in (
114
) (see
the proof of Proposition 4.17 of
[
F
]
).
Therefore,

\left|\sigma_{-t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{2})\right|=\left|\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\right|
.
More precisely, by Lemma
78
,

\sigma_{t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{1})=e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\left|\det(P)\right|,\quad\sigma_{-t_{1},0}^{\tau}(x_{2})=e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{2})}\cdot\left|\det(P)\right|.

(126)

Then on the one hand we have that to leading order

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi_{-},\nu,\lambda}(x_{2,\lambda},x_{1,\lambda})_{l}

(127)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\tau}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{2})}\cdot\left|\det(P)\right|

\displaystyle\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot\frac{A_{x_{2},x_{1}}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right)}{\pi^{d-1}}.

On the other hand, given (
125
), taking the complex conjugate of the expansion for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}
we also have

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}}

(128)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}}{\tau}}\cdot e^{-\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\left|\det(P)\right|

\displaystyle\cdot\chi(t_{1})\cdot\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot\frac{\overline{A_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right)}}{\pi^{d-1}}.

Therefore,

e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot A_{x_{2},x_{1}}(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau))\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{-t_{1}}(x_{2})}=e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot\overline{e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot A_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau))}.

If

A_{\chi}^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2}):=e^{\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}}\cdot A_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\mathbf{v}^{t}),

we conclude that
A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
is
independent of
\mathbf{v}^{t}
, and this implies the
stated equalities.
Setting

\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{0}
, one obtains

A^{\prime}_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})=A_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
.

∎

Returning to (
97
),
again in view of (
126
)
we obtain that
to leading order

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

(129)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{1}{\tau}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\right)},

where

\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})
and

\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}

are as in Definition
23

(here
g_{l}=\kappa_{l}\,h_{1}
).

Let us now consider the lower order terms in the
asymptotic expansion of
(
97
), hence in
(
104
).
For each
k
,
arguing as in (
108
) and
(
109
) one verifies that

\mathcal{I}_{k}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}
in (
105
)
is a linear combination of integrals of the form

\displaystyle\theta_{1}^{a}\,\mathbf{v}_{1}^{A}\,\theta_{2}^{a^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{v}_{2}^{A^{\prime}}

(130)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\left[\mathbf{u}^{C}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}\big)-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{(l)},\mathbf{u}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|(\mathbf{v}^{t}_{1})^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}\right]}\right.
