\displaystyle\left.\cdot\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\left[e^{\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}}\cdot\xi^{D}\right]\right],

where
a,\,a^{\prime}\geq 0
,
A,A^{\prime},C,D\geq 0
are multi-indexes and

a+a^{\prime}+|A|+|A^{\prime}|+|C|+|D|
has the same parity as
k

and is
\leq 3\,k
.

Let us consider the inner
\xi
-integral in (
130
).
Arguing as for (
110
), we can transfer integration
to
T^{v}X^{\tau}\cong\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}
, and conclude that, up to
some multiplicative constant factor, the
\xi
-integral
is given by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\mathbf{a}^{D}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{a}^{(l)},(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{a}^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}\right]}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{a}

(131)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{D^{\prime}+D^{\prime\prime}=D}\beta_{D^{\prime},D^{\prime\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{D^{\prime\prime}}\,e^{-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\mathbf{b}^{D^{\prime}}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\left[\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{b},(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{b}\right\|^{2}\right]}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{a},

for certain
\beta_{D^{\prime},D^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{C}
.

On the other hand, for any
\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}

for certain constants we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,\mathbf{b}^{D^{\prime}}\,e^{\imath\,\mathbf{b}\cdot\mathbf{r}-\frac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{b}\|^{2}}\,\mathbf{d}\mathbf{b}=\sum_{L\geq 0}c_{L}\mathbf{r}^{L}\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{r}\|^{2}},

(132)

where
c_{L}\neq 0
only if
L\leq D^{\prime}
, and

|L|
and
|D^{\prime}|
have the same parity.
We conclude that (
131
) can be rewritten
in the form

\sum_{D^{\prime}+L}\tilde{\beta}_{D^{\prime},L}\cdot\mathbf{u}^{D^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathbf{v}_{1}^{L}e^{-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t})^{(l)}+\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}},

(133)

where
\tilde{\beta}_{D^{\prime},L}\neq 0
only if
D^{\prime}+L\leq D
and

|D^{\prime}|+|L|
has the same parity as
|D|
.

If we insert (
133
) in (
130
), assuming that

\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}
,
we conclude that the latter is in turn a linear combination of integrals of the form

\displaystyle\theta_{1}^{a}\,\mathbf{v}_{1}^{A}\,\theta_{2}^{a^{\prime}}\,\mathbf{v}_{2}^{A^{\prime}}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}_{1}\right\|^{2}}

(134)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\left[\mathbf{u}^{C}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\big)-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}\right]}\right],

where
a+a^{\prime}+|A|+|A^{\prime}|+|C|
has the same parity as
k
,
and is
\leq 3\,k
.

Let us define
\mathcal{L}:\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

by setting

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\left[\mathbf{u}^{C}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\psi_{2}\big(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\big)-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}\right]}\right].

The exponent in the integrand has the form

-\left\langle\mathbf{u},\frac{1}{\tau}J_{0}B^{-1}J_{0}\mathbf{v}\right\rangle+\imath\,\left\langle\mathbf{u},\frac{1}{\tau}\,J_{0}\,B^{-1}\,\mathbf{v}\right\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{u},R\,\mathbf{u}\rangle,

where
R
is a certain complex symmetric matrix with positive definite real part.
It follows that
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{v})
is a linear
combination of terms of the form

\mathbf{v}^{D}\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\langle\mathbf{v},\tilde{R}^{-1}\,\mathbf{v}\right\rangle},

where
\tilde{R}
is another complex symmetric matrix with
\Re(\tilde{R})\gg 0
,
|D|\leq|C|
and
|D|
,
|C|
have the same parity.
If we finally insert
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}
, we obtain
the claimed statement.

∎

Remark 80
.

The same arguments for the lower order term can be applied to the
action-free case (with minor modifications), thus completing the proof
of Theorem
15
.

Proof of Statement 2 of Theorem
25
.

In this case, (
112
) is

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})_{l}

(135)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}^{h}\right)+g_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}^{h}\right)-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right)+g_{0}(B\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|B\mathbf{u}\right\|^{2}.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\langle\mathbf{V}(\tau),\,A_{l}\,\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{u}^{t}\,R\,\mathbf{u},

where
\mathbf{V}(\tau)^{\dagger}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)^{\dagger}&\mathbf{v}_{1}^{h}(\tau)^{\dagger}&\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)^{\dagger}\end{pmatrix}
, and
A_{l}
and
R
are complex matrices, with
R=R^{\dagger}

and
\Re(R)\gg 0
.
Thus we may rewrite (
111
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}

(136)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,e^{-\imath\,\langle\mathbf{V}(\tau),\,A_{l}\,\mathbf{u}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{u}^{t}\,R\,\mathbf{u}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d-1}}{\mathrm{det}(R)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V}(\tau),D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}(\tau)\rangle},

for a certain matrix
D=D^{\dagger}
. The ambiguity in the choice of the square root of the determinant of the matrix
R
is resolved using analytic continuation arguments, as in Appendix A of
[
D
]
.
Therefore, after performing the integral, in place of
(
129
) we obtain

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

(138)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot\mathrm{det}(R)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{1}{\tau}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V}(\tau),D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}(\tau)\rangle}.

Furthermore, for
\theta_{j}=0
and
\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{0}

we obtain an asymptotic expansion for
\Pi_{k,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

which must agree with the one obtained setting

\theta_{j}=0
,
\mathbf{v}_{j}=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}=\mathbf{0}
in the previous case.
Hence
\det(R)=1
.

In order to verify that
\Re(D_{l})\gg 0
, let us consider the
special case where
\nu
is the
trivial representation and
\theta_{j}=0
.
Then
\mathcal{B}_{\nu}(x_{1},x_{2})_{l}=1
and
so

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})

(139)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t_{1}}(x_{1})}\cdot\mathcal{F}_{\chi,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\,\langle\mathbf{V}(\tau),D_{l}\,\mathbf{V}(\tau)\rangle}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\right)\right]

uniformly for
\|\mathbf{V}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
.

On the other hand, in view of Definition
18
, we have

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\max\left\{\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{1,\lambda},Z^{\tau}\right),\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{2,\lambda},Z^{\tau}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\,\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\max\left\{\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|\right\},

and

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda}^{G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda}^{G}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2\,\sqrt{\lambda}}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{h}\right\|.

Let us set
\delta_{\lambda}:=\max\{\delta^{\prime}_{\lambda},\,\delta^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}\}
. If
\|\mathbf{V}\|=\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}

for some
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
,
we conclude that
\delta_{\lambda}\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon-1/2}
,
and therefore

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)
by Theorem
7
.
However, this will be true only if every summand
in (
139
) is rapidly
decreasing, i.e. if
\Re(D_{l})\gg 0
for every
l
.

The remaining arguments are a repetition of previous ones.

∎

4.4
Proof of Theorem
28

We now consider near-diagonal scaling asymptotics, thus with

x_{1}=x_{2}=x
, and assume

\chi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}\big((-\epsilon,\epsilon)\big)

for some
\epsilon>0
suitably small; then
t_{1}=0
,

x_{12}=x
, and
B=I_{2d-2}
(the identity matrix)
in (
25
).
Furthermore, we may
take
h_{1}=e
in (
39
).
We adopt the notation (
27
).

Proof of Theorem
28
.

The exponent (
112
) becomes

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})_{l}

(140)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{u}^{h}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)+\psi_{2}\big(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau)\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{h}_{l}+A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{vt}_{l},

where

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{h}_{l}:=-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u}^{h}\right)

(141)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|^{2}+g_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u}^{h}\right),

\displaystyle A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{vt}_{l}:=-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}

(142)

\displaystyle-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}-\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}-\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2},

By standard Gaussian integrations, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2\,(d-1-d_{G})}}e^{A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{h}_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}

(143)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}\right\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau)\right)+\frac{1}{4}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)}+\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2\,(d-1-d_{G})}}e^{-\|\mathbf{a}\|^{2}-\imath\,\omega_{0}\big(\mathbf{v}_{1}^{h}(\tau)^{(l)}-\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{a}\big)}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{d-1-d_{G}}\,e^{\psi_{2}\left(\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1}(\tau)^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}(\tau)\right)},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2\,d_{G}}}e^{A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})^{vt}_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{v}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}=e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}

(144)

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\left[e^{-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}-\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}e^{-\imath\,\omega_{0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{v},\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{v}\right\|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{v}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}-\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}

(145)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi^{d_{G}}\,e^{-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}(\tau)\right\|^{2}}.

Hence, recalling (
107
), (
111
)
reduces to

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2})_{l}

(146)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,e^{A(\mathbf{v}_{1}(\tau),\mathbf{v}_{2}(\tau),\mathbf{u})_{l}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\pi^{d-1}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}+\psi_{2}\left((\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}\right)\right]}.

Since
\sigma^{\tau}_{0,0}=1
by (
126
),
the leading order term in
(
104
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\big[I_{\lambda}(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2},\xi,\mathbf{u})_{l}\big]

(147)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle e^{\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,\frac{\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}}{\tau}}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi^{2}\,\tau^{2})^{d-1}}\cdot\sigma_{0,0}^{\tau}(x_{1})\cdot\chi(0)\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{(2\,\pi)^{d_{G}/2}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}\cdot\tau^{d-1+d_{G}/2}\,\pi^{d-1}\,e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}+\psi_{2}\left((\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}\right)\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}+\psi_{2}\left((\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}\right)\right]}\cdot\frac{\lambda^{2d-3}}{(2\,\pi\,\tau)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}}\cdot\frac{\chi(0)\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\right)}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}.

Inserting (
147
)
in (
97
),
we finally obtain that to leading order

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1,\lambda},x_{2,\lambda})_{l}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\cdot\frac{\chi(0)\cdot\overline{\Xi_{\nu}\left(\kappa_{l}\right)}}{r\cdot V_{eff}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{1}{\tau}\,\left[\imath\,\sqrt{\lambda}\,(\theta_{1}-\theta_{2})-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}-\left\|\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}+\psi_{2}\left((\mathbf{v}^{h}_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}^{h}_{2}\right)\right]},

The argument for the lower order terms is the same as for
Theorem
25
.

∎

5
Scaling asymptotics for
P_{k,\nu,\lambda}

The previous techniques may be applied to the
asymptotics of the complexifications of the equivariant eigenfunctions
of the positive Laplacian operator
\Delta
on
(M,\kappa)

(recall Theorem
1
).
In this section we describe the necessary adaptations to the
previous arguments.

As in (
3
),
let the
\mu_{j}
’s be the distinct
eigenvalues of
\sqrt{\Delta}
.
Let us choose for every
j
a real orthonormal
basis
(\varphi_{j,k})_{k=1}^{\ell_{j}^{\prime}}
of the eigenspace
W_{j}
of
\mu_{j}
.
Similarly, for every
j
such that
the equivariant component

W_{j,\nu}
of
W_{j}
is non-zero
let

(\varphi_{j,\nu,k})_{k=1}^{\ell_{j,\nu}^{\prime}}
be
a real orthonormal basis of
W_{j,\nu}
.

Let us recall the following basic facts; a detailed discussion
may be found in
[
Z-2012
]
,
[
Z-2013
]
,
[
Z-2020
]
.

1.

The wave operator

U(t)=e^{\imath\,t\,\sqrt{\Delta}}:L^{2}(M)\rightarrow L^{2}(M)

at time
t\in\mathbb{R}

is the unitary operator with distributional kernel

U(t)(m,n)=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}e^{\imath\,\mu_{j}\,t}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\,\varphi_{j,k}(m)\cdot\varphi_{j,k}(n).

2.

For every
\tau>0
, the distributional kernel

U(\imath\,\tau)(m,n):=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}e^{-\mu_{j}\,\tau}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\,\varphi_{j,k}(m)\cdot\varphi_{j,k}(n)

is globally real-analytic on
M\times M
;
there exists
\tau_{1}\in(0,\tau_{0}]
such that if

\tau\in(0,\tau_{1}]
then
U(\imath\,\tau)

admits a
holomorphic extension on
M^{\tau}\times M
in the first variable,

\widetilde{U}(\imath\,\tau)(x,n)=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}e^{-\mu_{j}\,\tau}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\,\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}(x)\cdot\varphi_{j,k}(n).

3.

For
\tau\in(0,\tau_{1})
,
the restriction of
\widetilde{U}(\imath\,\tau)

to
X^{\tau}\times M
is the distributional kernel of an operator

\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}:\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(X^{\tau}),

which is a Fourier integral operator with complex phase
of degree
-(d-1)/4
;

4.

\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}
controls the complexification of the eigenfunctions

\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}
:
leaving restriction to
X^{\tau}
implicit,

\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}=e^{\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}(\varphi_{j,k}).

5.

For every
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}
determines a
continuous isomorphism of Sobolev spaces

\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}:W^{s}(M)\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{s+\frac{d-1}{4}}(X^{\tau}),

where
W^{s}(M)

is the
s
-th Sobolev space of
M
, and
\mathcal{O}^{k}(X^{\tau})
is the intersection
of the
k
-th Sobolev space of
X^{\tau}
with the space of
CR (generalized) functions on
X^{\tau}
.

6.

The composition

U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau):=\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}\circ U(t)\circ{\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}}^{*}

is a Fourier integral operator with complex phase of degree

-(d-1)/2
on
X^{\tau}
, whose distributional kernel
admits the spectral description

\displaystyle U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)(x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}e^{(-2\,\tau+\imath\,t)\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\,\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}(x)\,\overline{\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}(y)}

(148)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}e^{\imath\,t\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k=1}^{\ell^{\prime}_{j}}\,\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}\left(\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,k}\right)(x)\,\overline{\mathfrak{P}^{\tau}(\varphi)_{j,k}(y)};

we shall also use the notation

U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau,x,y)=U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)(x,y)
.

7.

For every
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)

determines a continuous isomorphism
of CR Sobolev spaces

U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau):\mathcal{O}^{s}(X^{\tau})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}^{s+\frac{d-1}{2}}(X^{\tau}).

8.

Let us denote by
\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}
the zeroth order
Fourier integral operator on
X^{\tau}
having distributional kernel

\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}(x,y):=\Pi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(x),y\right);

then on

X^{\tau}
there exist a smoothly varying pseudodifferential operator

Q^{\tau}_{t}
of degree

-(d-1)/2

and a smoothly varying operator
R^{\tau}_{t}
with
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
kernel such that

U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)=\Pi^{\tau}\circ Q^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}+R^{\tau}_{t}.

(149)

9.

In a conic neighoburhood of the symplectic cone
\Sigma^{\tau}
,

Q^{\tau}_{t}
admits the following microlocal description. Let us set

D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}:=\imath\,\upsilon_{\sqrt{\rho}}
. Then there exists
a polyhomogeneous classical symbol on
X^{\tau}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
of the form

\gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x,r)\sim\sum_{j\geq 0}\gamma^{\tau}_{t,j}(x)\,r^{-\frac{d-1}{2}-j},

(150)

such that
Q^{\tau}_{t}\sim\gamma^{\tau}_{t}\big(x,D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}\big)
.

10.

Similarly to
(
58
),
the leading coefficient in (
150
) is

\gamma^{\tau}_{t,0}(x)=\tau^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\cdot e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t}(x)}\cdot\langle\sigma_{J}^{(x)},\sigma_{J_{t}}^{(x)}\rangle^{-1}

(151)

for a certain smooth function
\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t}:X^{\tau}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
. The additional factor
\tau^{\frac{d-1}{2}}

with respect to the computation of Zelditch (see e.g.
[
Z-2012
]
)
is due to our choice of volume form, which affects the construction
of
{P^{\tau}}^{*}
(see the discussion in
[
P-2024
]
).

11.

The principal symbol of

D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
along
\Sigma^{\tau}
(or, equivalently, of
\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}
) is

\sigma(D^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}})\left(x,v\,\alpha_{x}^{\tau}\right)=v\,\tau\qquad(v>0).

Proof of Theorem
29
.

We are interested in the asymptotics of the kernel

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\mu_{j})\,e^{-2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,k}(x)\,\overline{\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,k}(y)}\qquad(x,y\in X^{\tau}),

and more generally of its equivariant version

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,y):=\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\mu_{j})\,e^{-2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(x)\,\overline{\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}(y)}.

(152)

The analysis run parallel to the one conducted for

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
.

More precisely, the following analogue of (
55
)
describes the relation between
P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}

and
U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau)

in (
148
)
is given by :

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(x,y)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau,x,y)\,\mathrm{d}t;

(153)

furthermore, operatorially we have the analogue of
(
18
):

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}=P_{\nu}\circ P^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}.

(154)

Arrguing as in the proof of Theorem

7
,
with
U_{\mathbb{C}}(t+2\,\imath\,\tau,x,y)
in place of

U^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}(t;x,y)

and (
149
) in place of (
56
),
we obtain in place of (
64
):

\displaystyle P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(155)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,\chi(t)\,\left(\Pi^{\tau}\circ Q^{\tau}_{t}\circ\Pi^{\tau}_{-t}\right)\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),x_{2}\right)\right].

The arguments in the proof of Theorem
7

apply, except that the leading term of the amplitude
has been multiplied by a factor

\tau^{(d-1)/2}\,(u\,\tau)^{-(d-1)/2}
and the
unitary factor
e^{\imath\,\tilde{\theta}^{\tau}_{t}(x)}

in (
151
)
replaces
e^{\imath\,\theta^{\tau}_{t}(x)}
.
In view of the rescaling
u\mapsto\lambda\,u
, this entails a
further factor
\lambda^{-(d-1)/2}
.
Furthermore, at the critical point
(
101
) of the phase the product

u\,\tau=1

∎

6
Near-graph uniform asymptotic expansions

Given
(x_{1},x_{2})\in Z^{\tau}\times Z^{\tau}
, we have defined

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})\subseteq G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
in
Remark
59
.

Lemma 81
.

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
has the following properties:

1.

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})\neq\emptyset
if and only if
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
;

2.

for any
(x_{1},x_{2})\in Z^{\tau}\times Z^{\tau}
and any
neighbourhood
S
of
\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
in
G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
,
there exists a neighbourhood

Z^{\prime}
of
(x_{1},x_{2})
in
Z^{\tau}\times Z^{\tau}
such that

(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\in Z^{\prime}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\subseteq S.

Proof.

The first statement is obvious by definition. If the second was false,
for any
j=1,2,\ldots
there would exist

(y_{j}^{\prime},y_{j}^{\prime\prime})\in Z^{\tau}\times Z^{\tau}
having distance
<1/j
from

(x_{1},x_{2})
and
(g_{j},t_{j})\in\Sigma_{\chi}(y_{j}^{\prime},y_{j}^{\prime\prime})
having distance from
\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})

no less than
\epsilon_{0}
, for some fixed
\epsilon_{0}>0
.
By compactness, we may assume without loss that
g_{j}\rightarrow g_{\infty}\in G
and
t_{j}\rightarrow t_{\infty}
in
\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
.
By continuity,

y_{j}^{\prime}=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{j}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{j}}(y_{j}^{\prime\prime})\quad\Rightarrow\quad x_{1}=\mu^{\tau}_{g_{\infty}}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t_{\infty}}(x_{2})\quad\Rightarrow\quad(g_{\infty},t_{\infty})\in\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2}).

hence
(g_{j},t_{j})\rightarrow(g_{\infty},t_{\infty})\in\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
, absurd.
∎

Proof of Theorem
30
.

To begin with, we consider the asymptotics at fixed points
(that is, with no rescaling). Let us choose

(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathfrak{X}^{\tau}_{\chi}
, hence
satisfying (
38
): there exist

(g,t)\in G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
such that

x_{1}=\mu^{\tau}_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x_{2})
.
We may assume that the possible pairs
(g,t)
can be listed as in
(
39
).
We have

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

(156)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\lambda^{2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{G}\,\mathrm{d}V_{G}(g)\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y)\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{\nu}\left(g^{-1}\right)\,\chi(t)\,e^{\imath\,\lambda\,\left[u\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y\right)+v\,\psi^{\tau}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{-t}(y),x_{2}\right)-t\right]}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\varrho_{1}(g,y)\,\varrho_{2}(t,y)\,s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{g^{-1}}(x_{1}),y,\lambda\,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\left(y,x_{2},\lambda\,v\right)\right].

Let us multiply the integrand
in (
156
)
by the unrescaled cut-off

\gamma^{\mathbb{R}}\left(t-t_{1}\right)\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\gamma^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\log_{G}\left(g\,h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\right)\right),

(157)

where
G_{x_{1}}=\{\kappa_{1},\ldots,\kappa_{r_{x_{1}}}\}
.
Thus integration in
G\times\mathbb{R}

has been restricted to a small
but fixed neighbourhood of
\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
, and only a rapidly
decreasing contribution to the asymptotics
of (
156
) is lost.
The same will then be true for the asymptotics of

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})
for any

(x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2}^{\prime})
in a fixed small neighbourhood of
(x_{1},x_{2})
, as

\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})
is then contained in a small
neighbourhood of
\Sigma_{\chi}(x_{1},x_{2})
by Lemma

81
.

Thus, uniformly on an open neighbourhood of
(x_{1},x_{2})
we have

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})_{l},

where for each
l
(with the change of variable

t\mapsto t_{1}+t
) we have

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2})_{l}

(158)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t_{1}}\,\lambda^{2}\,\frac{\dim(\nu)}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{\mathfrak{g}}\,\mathrm{d}\xi\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}\theta\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}u\,\int_{0}^{+\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\left[e^{\imath\,\lambda\,\Psi_{x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}}\,\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}\right].

At
(x_{1},x_{2})

we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{x_{1},x_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}:=-u\,\theta+v\,(\theta+\tau\,t)-t

(159)

\displaystyle+\imath\,u\,\left[\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,\theta^{2}-\psi_{2}\left(-\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)},\mathbf{v}\right)\right]+\imath\,v\,\left[\frac{1}{4\,\tau^{2}}\,(\theta+\tau\,t)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\big\|B\mathbf{v}\big\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+R_{3}(\mathbf{v},\xi,\theta,t)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2},t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}

(160)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Xi_{\nu}\left(h_{1}^{-1}\,\kappa_{l}^{-1}\,e^{-\xi}\right)\,\chi\left(t+t_{1}\right)\cdot\mathcal{V}\left(\theta,\mathbf{u}\right)\cdot\gamma(t,\theta,\xi,\mathbf{u})\cdot f_{1}(v)\cdot f_{2}(u)

\displaystyle\cdot s^{\tau}\left(\mu^{\tau}_{(e^{\xi}\kappa_{l})^{-1}}(x),y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}),\lambda\,u\right)\,r^{\tau}_{t}\big(y_{\lambda}(\theta,\mathbf{u}),x,\lambda\,v\big),

where
\gamma(t,\theta,\xi,\mathbf{u})

is a product of unrescaled cut-offs and
f_{1},\,f_{2}
are as in Proposition
72
.
In particular, integration is restricted to a small neighbourhood
of the locus where
\theta=t=0
,
\mathbf{v}=\xi=0
, and
is compactly supported in
(u,v)
.

We have
\Im\Psi_{x_{1},x_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}\geq 0
, and

\Im\Psi_{x_{1},x_{2}}(t,v,\theta,u,\mathbf{v},\xi)_{l}=0
only if

\theta=t=0,\quad\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}},\quad\xi=0_{\mathfrak{g}}.

We have in addition a stationary point, if we also
impose
u=v=1/\tau
. Thus the only nonnegligible contribution
to the asymptotics may come from a neighourhood of

P_{0}:=(t_{0},v_{0},\theta_{0},u_{0},\mathbf{v}_{0},\xi_{0})=\left(0,\frac{1}{\tau},0,\frac{1}{\tau},\mathbf{0}_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}},0_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)

Let
{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}
denote the
(2d-2)\times d_{G}

matrix representing the injective linear map

\xi\mapsto\xi_{X^{\tau}}(x_{1})^{(l)}
with respect to the
given orthonormal basis in
\mathfrak{g}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\tau}_{x_{1}}
.
At the critical point, the Hessian of
\Psi_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\cdot)_{l}
is

H_{P_{0}}\big(\Psi_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\cdot)_{l}\big)=\begin{pmatrix}\imath/2&\tau&\imath/(2\,\tau^{2})&0&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}\\
\tau&0&1&0&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}\\
\imath/(2\,\tau^{2})&1&\imath/\tau^{3}&-1&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}\\
0&0&-1&0&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}&\mathbf{0}^{\dagger}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}&\mathcal{C}\end{pmatrix}

where

\mathcal{A}:=\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right),\quad\mathcal{B}:=\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,(I_{2d-2}+\imath\,J_{0})\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}},\quad\mathcal{C}:=\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}.

Here
J_{0}
is the standard complex structure on

\mathbb{C}^{d-1}\cong\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\oplus\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
.
Thus the Hessian matrix is in block diagonal form, and the
determinants of the two blocks are as follows.

First we have

\det\left(\frac{1}{\imath}\,\begin{pmatrix}\imath/2&\tau&\imath/(2\,\tau^{2})&0\\
\tau&0&1&0\\
\imath/(2\,\tau^{2})&1&\imath/\tau^{3}&-1\\
0&0&-1&0\end{pmatrix}\right)=\tau^{2}.

As to the second block, we have

\displaystyle\det\left(\frac{1}{\imath}\,\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
\mathcal{B}^{\dagger}&\mathcal{C}\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det\left(\frac{1}{\imath}\,\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)&\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,(I_{2d-2}+\imath\,J_{0})\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}\\
\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,(I_{2d-2}-\imath\,J_{0})&\frac{\imath}{\tau}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau^{2d-2+d_{G}}}\,\det\begin{pmatrix}\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)&(I_{2d-2}+\imath\,J_{0})\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}\\
{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,(I_{2d-2}-\imath\,J_{0})&{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau^{2d-2+d_{G}}}\,\det\begin{pmatrix}\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)&(I_{2d-2}+\imath\,J_{0})\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}\\
0&S\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{D_{x}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}-{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,(I_{2d-2}-\imath\,J_{0})\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\,(I_{2d-2}+\imath\,J_{0})\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}.

Thus
S=S^{\dagger}
, and the real part of
S
is

\displaystyle\Re(S)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x}^{l}}-{D_{x}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\,\,{D_{x}^{l}}

\displaystyle-{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,J_{0}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\,J_{0}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}.

We have (since
B
is a symplectic matrix)

\displaystyle\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)\,J_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{0}+B^{\dagger}\,B\,J_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{0}+J_{0}\,\left(B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{0}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+\left(B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\right).

Hence, taking inverses we get

J_{0}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}=\left(I_{2d-2}+\left(B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\,J_{0}.

Thus

\displaystyle\Re(S)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}-{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\,\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}

\displaystyle-{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+\left(B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\,J_{0}\,J_{0}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,\left[I_{2d-2}-\left(I_{2d-2}+B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\right]\,\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}

\displaystyle+{D_{x_{1}}^{l}}^{\dagger}\,\left(I_{2d-2}+\left(B^{\dagger}\,B\right)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\,{D_{x_{1}}^{l}},

whence
\Re(S)\gg 0
.

Hence
P_{0}
is a non-degenerate stationary point;
by the complex stationary phase lemma of
[
MS
]
,
we obtain
an asymptotic expansion for
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1},x_{2})

which must agree with our previous derivation.
Moreover, since the
complex stationary phase holds with parameters, we can replace
(x_{1},x_{2})

by a general
(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})
varying in
some small open neighbourhood of
(x_{1},x_{2})
,
and obtain an
asymptotic expansions

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\sim\sum_{l=1}^{r_{x_{1}}}\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2}^{\prime})_{l},

(161)

but for some
l
the corresponding stationary
point (accounting for the expansion of the
l
-th summand
in (
161
))
might cease to be real; this happens
when
(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}
but
r_{x_{1}^{\prime}}<r_{x_{1}}
, or when

(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})\not\in\mathfrak{X}_{\chi}^{\tau}
. In this case,

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime})_{l}=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)
.

In particular, let us now replace

x_{j}
by, say,
x_{j}^{\prime}:=x_{j}+(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t})

for some nearby
x_{j}^{\prime}=x_{j}+(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})
, with
(\theta_{j},\mathbf{v}_{j})
suitably small.
By non-degeneracy, the critical point will vary smoothly,
except that it
will generically move to the complex domain, which accounts for the exponential decay in the scaling asymptotics. Nonetheless, the asymptotic expansions will still hold, and if we pair this with the scaling asymptotics in the previous theorems,
we obtain the statement of Theorem
30
.

∎

Remark 82
.

In our situation,
R_{3}
and

L_{\nu,l,s}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot\right),\,K_{\nu,l,s}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot\right)

are complex valued real-analytic functions (meaning that their real and imaginary components are real-analytic).
Hence there are expansions of the form

\displaystyle e^{\lambda\,R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{j}}{j!}\,R_{3}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\frac{\lambda^{j}}{j!}\,\left(\sum_{a\geq 0}P_{3+a}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\lambda^{j}\,\sum_{a\geq 0}P_{3j+a}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)

where
P_{k}\left(\cdot\right)
is a generic homogeneous polynomial
of degree
k
and, say,

L_{\nu,l,s}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)=\sum_{k\geq 0}L_{\nu,l,s,k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right),

where each
L_{\nu,l,s,k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\cdot\right)

is a homogeneous polynomial of total degree
k
.
Upon rescaling, the product of these expansions
gives rise to terms of the form

\displaystyle\lambda^{j-s-\frac{k}{2}-\frac{3j}{2}-\frac{a}{2}}\,P_{3j+a}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)\,L_{\nu,l,s,k}\left(x_{1},x_{2};\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}\,(j+2s+a+k)}\,\tilde{P}_{k+3j+a}\left(\theta_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\theta_{2},\mathbf{v}_{2}^{t}\right)

where
\tilde{P}_{k+3j+a}
is homogeneous of total degree

3j+a+k
.
We obtain the same conclusions as before, that is general
terms of the form
\lambda^{-k/2}\,P_{r}
with

r\leq 3\,k
and
r-k
even.

7
Applications

7.1
The equivariant Weyl law for

\mathfrak{D}^{\tau}_{\sqrt{\rho}}

Proof of Theorem
31
.

Let
\chi
have sufficiently small support and satisfy

\hat{\chi}>0
and
\chi(0)>0
.
Recalling (
18
),

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}

(162)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\Pi^{\tau}_{j,\nu}(x,x)\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\left[\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\Pi^{\tau}_{j,\nu}(x,x)\right]\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{X^{\tau}}\,\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x,x)\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(x).

Let us fix
C>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
.
By Theorem
7
, we only loose a rapidly decreasing
contribution in (
162
), if
integration is restricted to a shrinking
tubular neighourhood of
Z^{\tau}
of radius

C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}
.
Furthermore, any such neighbourhood
may be locally parametrized
using smoothly varying systems of NHLC’s centered
at moving points
x\in Z^{\tau}
.

More precisely, in view of Remark

19
,
for any
x\in Z^{\tau}
we may find an open neighbourhood

Z^{\prime}\subseteq Z^{\tau}
of
x
and a smoothly varying family of
normal Heisenberg coordinates centered at points
x^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime}
, such that
the map

(x^{\prime},\mathbf{v}^{t})\in Z^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}\mapsto x^{\prime}+\mathbf{v}^{t}\in X^{\tau}

(163)

is a diffeomorphism onto a neighbourhood of
x
in
X^{\tau}
(here we
use normal Heisenberg coordinates at each
x^{\prime}
to identify

\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}\cong\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}_{t}\cong T_{x^{\prime}}^{t}X^{\tau}
).
If we pass to the rescaled local parametrization

x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}):=x+\frac{\mathbf{v}^{t}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\qquad(x^{\prime}\in Z^{\prime},\,\mathbf{v}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}),

(164)

then uniformly for

\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\|\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}

there are asymptotic expansion

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}),x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot\frac{\dim(\nu)^{2}}{V_{eff}(x_{1})}

(165)

\displaystyle\cdot e^{-\frac{2}{\tau}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}}\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k,l,\nu}\left(x;\mathbf{v}^{t}\right)\right],

where
F_{k,l,\nu}(x;\cdot)
is a polynomial of degree
\leq 3\,k
and
parity
k
.

Let us choose an open cover of
Z^{\tau}
by open sets
Z^{\prime}_{j}
as above,
and a subordinate partition of unity
\beta_{j}
on
Z^{\tau}
;
we can then express the latter integral
in (
162
) as a sum of local contributions.
For notational simplicity, we shall force notation and
leave the partition
\{\beta_{j}\}
implicit.
We obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}

(166)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\int_{Z^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{Z^{\tau}}(x)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x+\mathbf{v}^{t},x+\mathbf{v}^{t})\,\,\mathcal{V}(x+\mathbf{v}^{t})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda^{-d_{G}/2}\,\int_{Z^{\tau}}\,\mathrm{d}V_{Z^{\tau}}(x)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}),x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t})\right)\,\,\mathcal{V}(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}))\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}^{t}\right]

where
\mathrm{d}V_{Z^{\tau}}
is the Riemannian volume form
on
Z^{\tau}
for the restricted metric,
and
\mathcal{V}(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{0}))=1
for every
x\in X^{\tau}
.
Integration in
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}^{t}

is now over an expanding ball of radius

O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)
in
\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}
.
By multiplying the asymptotic expansion of Theorem

28
with the Taylor expansion of

\mathcal{V}(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}))
, we obtain an asymptotic
expansion for the integrand in (
166
)
of the form

\displaystyle\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}),x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t})\right)\,\,\mathcal{V}(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}))

(167)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\cdot\frac{\dim(\nu)^{2}}{V_{eff}(x)}\cdot e^{-\frac{2}{\tau}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k,\nu}\left(x;\mathbf{v}^{t}\right)\right],

where
F_{k,\nu}(x;\cdot)
is a polynomial of degree
\leq 3\,k
and
parity
k
. The expansion (
167
)
may be integrated term by term;
we obtain an asymptotic expansion
for (
166
) of the form

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\hat{\chi}(\lambda-\lambda_{j})\,\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}

(168)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\,\lambda^{-d_{G}/2}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\int_{Z^{\tau}}\,\frac{1}{V_{eff}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}V_{Z^{\tau}}(x)\right)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{G}}}\,e^{-\frac{2}{\tau}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}^{t}\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k}\,\mathfrak{f}_{k,\nu}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d-1}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k}\,\mathfrak{f}_{k,\nu}\right].

As in
[
P-2024
]
, we shall now follow a standard
argument from spectral analysis (see e.g.
[
GrSj
]
).
Let

f_{\lambda}:\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)

be defined by

f_{\lambda}(s,t):=\hat{\chi}(t)\cdot H(\lambda-s-t),

(169)

where
H
is the Heaviside function.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be the Lebsesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
,
and let
\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{\nu}
be the positive
measure on
\mathbb{R}
given by

\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}:=\sum_{j\geq 1}\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\,\delta_{\lambda_{j}};

(170)

here
\delta_{a}
is the delta measure at
a\in\mathbb{R}
.
Let us endow
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
with the product
meaure
\mathcal{P}^{\tau}_{\nu}\times\mathcal{L}
.
By the Fubini Theorem,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\,\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\right]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right].

(171)

The right hand side in (
171
) is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]

(172)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda-s}\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\,\hat{\chi}(t-s)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\,\hat{\chi}(t-\lambda_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\,\left[\sum_{j}\,\hat{\chi}(t-\lambda_{j})\cdot\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

In view of (
168
),
we conclude
that as
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]

(173)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d-1}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\cdot\frac{\lambda}{d-d_{G}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k}\,F_{k,\nu}\left(x\right)\right].

On the other hand, the left hand side in (
171
)
is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\,\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{Q}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\right]

(174)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\left[\sum_{j}\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\,\,\hat{\chi}(t)\cdot H(\lambda-\lambda_{j}-t)\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\left[\sum_{\lambda_{j}\leq\lambda-t}\dim H^{\tau}(X)_{j,\nu}\,\right]\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda-t)\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\chi(0)\,\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\left[\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda-t)-\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)\right]\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t).

An adaptation of the argument in the proof of Lemma 70 of

[
P-2024
]
,
paired with (
168
), yields the estimate

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\left[\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda-t)-\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)\right]\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)=O\left(\lambda^{d-1-d_{G}}\right)

(175)

for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
.
We conclude

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d-1}\,(2\,\pi)}\,\cdot\frac{\lambda}{d-d_{G}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{d_{G}}}\cdot\frac{\tau}{d-d_{G}}\cdot\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\,\pi\,\tau}\right)^{d-d_{G}}\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{-1}\right)\right]

∎

7.2
Pointwise estimates on eigenfunctions

Proof of Proposition
32
.

If
x\not\in Z^{\tau}
, there exists an open neighbourhood

Y_{x}\subseteq X^{\tau}
of
x
such that

\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime})=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)

for
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, uniformly for
x^{\prime}\in Y_{x}
.
If
x\in X^{\tau}
, there exists similarly a neighbourhood
Y_{x}
where
the asymptotic expansion of Theorem
30

implies
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime})\leq C_{x}\,\lambda^{d-1-d_{G}/2}

for some constant
C_{x}
, uniformly for
x^{\prime}\in Y_{x}
.
The claim follows by the compactness of
X^{\tau}
.

∎

7.3
Equivariant operator norm estimates

We can now prove the equivariant version of the
operator norm estimate of Chang and Rabinowitz (
29
);
a similar statement may be proved with minor changes for

P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}
.

Proof of Theorem
34
.

By the
Shur-Young inequality,
for some positive constant
D^{\tau}_{p}

\left\|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}\rightarrow L^{q}}\leq D_{p}^{\tau}\,\left[\sup_{y\in X^{\tau}}\int_{X^{\tau}}\left|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)\right|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y^{\prime})\right]^{\frac{1}{r}},

(176)

where

\frac{1}{r}:=1-\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}.

Let us choose
C>0
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1/6)
.
By Theorem
7
,
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)
uniformly for

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,Z^{\tau}\right)\geq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
.
Thus we may assume without loss of generality that

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y,Z^{\tau}\right)\leq C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}
.
Any such
y
may be written in the form

y=x+\frac{\mathbf{v}^{t}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},\quad\text{where}\quad x\in Z^{\tau},\quad\mathbf{v}^{t}\in T^{t}_{x}X^{\tau},\quad\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\|\leq 2\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}.

(177)

Let
0<a<A
be such that

a\,\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\right)\leq\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x^{\prime}),\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x^{\prime\prime})\right)\leq A\,\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\right)

for all
x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\tau}
and
(g,t)\in G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
.

Lemma 83
.

Uniformly for
y
as in (
177
) and

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},x^{G\times\chi}\right)\geq 3\,A\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}.

(178)

we have
\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)=O\left(\lambda^{-\infty}\right)
.

Proof.

If (
178
) holds,
then for any
(g,t)\in G\times\mathrm{supp}(\chi)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y)\right)

(179)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)-\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y),\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)-\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(y),\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},\mu_{g}\circ\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}(x)\right)-2\,A\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}\geq A\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}},

whence

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}\left(y^{\prime},x^{G\times\chi}\right)\geq A\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-\frac{1}{2}}
.
The claim follows by Theorem
7
.
∎

Hence integration in (
176
) may be restricted to
the shrinking locus where (
178
) is satisfied.
Any
y^{\prime}\in X^{\tau}
satisfying (
178
) may
in turn be written uniquely
(for a given choice of smoothly varying normal NHLC’s)
in the form

y^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x)+\frac{\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{h}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

(180)

where
t\in\mathrm{supp}(\chi)
,
g\in G
and

\mathbf{u}^{t}\in T^{t}_{\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x)}X^{\tau},\quad\mathbf{u}^{h}\in T^{h}_{\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x)}X^{\tau},\quad\left\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\right\|,\,\left\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\right\|=O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right).

In view of statement 3. of Theorem
25
and
of the previous considerations on uniformity,
there exist constants
a,\,C_{\nu}>0
such that, uniformly
for all
such choices,

\displaystyle\left|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)\right|^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x)+\frac{\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{h}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},x+\frac{\mathbf{v}^{t}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)\right|^{r}

(181)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\nu}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{r\,(d-1-d_{G}/2)}\,e^{-a\,\frac{r}{\tau}\left(\|\mathbf{u}^{t}\|+\|\mathbf{u}^{h}\|^{2}+\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\|^{2}\right)}

Let us set

F_{r}(y):=\int_{X^{\tau}}\left|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)\right|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y^{\prime})\qquad(y\in X^{\tau}).

(182)

Using an unparametrized version of (
180
),
that is,
y^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\tau}_{t}\circ\mu^{\tau}_{g}(x)+\mathbf{u}^{t}+\mathbf{u}^{h}
,
we have

\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y^{\prime})=\mathcal{V}(g,t,\mathbf{u}^{t},\mathbf{u}^{h})\,\mathrm{d}g\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}
for some positive function

\mathcal{V}(g,t,\mathbf{u}^{t},\mathbf{u}^{h})
, and integration
in
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}
is on a shrinking ball of radius

O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}-1/2}\right)
. Applying the
rescaling in (
180
) yields

\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y^{\prime})=\lambda^{-(d-1-d_{G})-d_{G}/2}\,\mathcal{V}(g,t,\mathbf{0}^{\dagger},\mathbf{0}^{h})\,\mathrm{d}g\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}+L.O.T.
, where

L.O.T.
denotes lower order terms in
\lambda
, and integration in
in
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}^{h}
is now on an expanding ball of radius

O\left(\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\right)
.

Given this, we obtain from (
181
) that
uniformly for
x\in Z^{\tau}
and
\mathbf{v}^{t}\in T^{t}_{x}X^{\tau}
with

\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\|\leq 2\,C\,\lambda^{\epsilon^{\prime}}
we have
(for some constant
D_{\nu}>0
)

\displaystyle F_{r}\left(x+\frac{\mathbf{v}^{t}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D_{\nu,r}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{r\,\left(d-1-\frac{d_{G}}{2}\right)}\,\left(\frac{\tau}{\lambda}\right)^{d-1-\frac{d_{G}}{2}}

(183)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\nu,r}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{(r-1)\,(d-1-d_{G}/2)}.

Hence, uniformly in
y\in X^{\tau}
we have for some
constant
D_{\nu,r}^{\prime}>0
that

\displaystyle\left[\sup_{y\in X^{\tau}}\int_{X^{\tau}}\left|\Pi^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}(y^{\prime},y)\right|^{r}\,\mathrm{d}V_{X^{\tau}}(y^{\prime})\right]^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle D_{\nu,r}^{\prime}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)\,\left(d-1-\frac{d_{G}}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{\nu,r}^{\prime}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}\right)\,\left(d-1-\frac{d_{G}}{2}\right)}.

∎

7.4
Proof of Proposition
38
and Theorem
40

Proof of Proposition
38
.

In the setting of Theorem
31
,
in place of (
165
) we have

\displaystyle P^{\tau}_{\chi,\nu,\lambda}\left(x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t}),x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t})\right)

(184)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\left(\frac{1}{2\,\pi}\right)^{d-1-d_{G}/2}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{(d-1-d_{G})/2}\cdot\frac{\dim(\nu)^{2}}{V_{eff}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{-\frac{2}{\tau}\,\left\|\mathbf{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}}\cdot\left[\chi(0)+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k/2}\,F_{k,l,\nu}\left(x;\mathbf{v}^{t}\right)\right],

where
is
x_{\lambda}(\mathbf{v}^{t})
is
as in (
164
) and

F_{k,l,\nu}(x;\cdot)
is a polynomial of degree
\leq 3\,k
and
parity
k
.
The statement of the Theorem follows by integration.
∎

Proof of Theorem
40
.

In analogy with (
170
), let us define
the positive measure

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\tau}_{\nu}:=\sum_{j\geq 1}\Lambda_{j,\nu}\,\delta_{\mu_{j}}

(185)

where

\Lambda_{j,\nu}:=e^{-2\,\tau\,\mu_{j}}\,\sum_{k}\left\|\widetilde{\varphi}^{\tau}_{j,\nu,k}\right\|_{L^{2}(X^{\tau})}^{2}.

With
f_{\lambda}
as in (
169
),
we have as in (
172
)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]=\int_{-\infty}^{\lambda}\,\left[\sum_{j}\,\hat{\chi}(t-\mu_{j})\cdot\Lambda_{j,\nu}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t).

(186)

In view of Proposition
38
,
we conclude
that as
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\tau}_{\nu,\lambda}(s)\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\right]

(187)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{d_{G}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}}{(2\,\pi)^{d}}\,\left(\frac{\lambda}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d-1}{2}-d_{G}}\,\cdot\dim(\nu)^{2}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(Z^{\tau}/G)

\displaystyle\cdot\lambda\,\left[\dfrac{\chi(0)}{\frac{d+1}{2}-d_{G}}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,\lambda^{-k}\,\mathfrak{f}_{k}^{\prime\prime}\right].

Similarly, arguing as for (
174
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)\,\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,f_{\lambda}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}^{\tau}_{\nu}(s)\right]

(188)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\chi(0)\,\mathcal{P}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)+\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,\left[\mathcal{P}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda-t)-\mathcal{P}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)\right]\,\hat{\chi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{L}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\chi(0)\,\mathcal{P}_{\nu}^{\tau}(\lambda)+O\left(\lambda^{\frac{d-1}{2}-d_{G}}\right),

where the last equality follows from
Proposition
38
and the argument in Lemma 70 of
[
P-2024
]
.

By the Fubini Theorem,
(
188
) equals
(
187
); the claim follows.

∎

\Re,\,\Im

32.

The Fourier transform of
f\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
is

\hat{f}(\lambda)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\,\pi}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\,e^{-\imath\,\lambda\,t}\,f(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

33.

\mathrm{dist}_{X^{\tau}}:X^{\tau}\times X^{\tau}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:
the Riemannian distance function
on
X^{\tau}
.

34.

\bullet^{\dagger}
indicates the transpose of a matrix or a vector,
\bullet^{*}
indicates the conjugate transpose of a matrix.
